#### M1 - Analyzer phase A: page, header and address

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M5
Identity History: M5 to M1 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #39 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/39)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

The Analyzer is the retrieval application's independent page (`archViewer.html`, `viewer.js`, `viewer.css`), reached through the console's `analyzer.html` entry and Browse PVs links. Its M1 foundation provides the shared site header, console navigation, time zone and profile choices, common PV and time selection, and the working Trend view. Seven other analysis tabs explain their unavailable status. Signals, Axes, Options, Data and Export contain visibility, per-unit ranges, shared drawing options, existing statistics, CSV and Copy link. The address preserves the common selection, preferences, options, projection and ranges. Advanced interactions and analysis belong to M2-M5. M1 is complete with the verification and landing evidence below; Pages deployment is verified after the release merge under M21 / Release Verification 7.

##### Scope

Phase A of `docs/design-analyzer.md` (D5): the analyzer as a page of the console's family, with nothing of the analysis yet.

- The current composition proposal uses eight analysis tabs (Trend, Distribution, Correlation, Dependency, Spectrum, Compare, Waveform, Image), common PV selection and time range above them, and a panel with Signals, Axes, Options, Data and Export. `docs/design-analyzer-ui.md` specifies the complete interface across M1-M5; `work/analyzer-mockup/index.html` demonstrates it with synthetic examples and a separate M1 foundation mode. The time-range controls appear once with the common selection above the analysis tabs; unit-specific Y ranges are configured in Axes (Decision 2026-11-04). The composition, configuration panels and unavailable-tool presentation are accepted for the M1 foundation (Decision 2026-11-04). The mockup does not implement or verify the product's analysis tools.
- The independent service with the header of the console: the site header and logo, the same menu as the console pages whose items navigate to the console pages at `mgmtURL`, the time zone choice of M67 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) (its step 5 moves here), the existing display-profile choice, and the Analyzer entry marked on both sides. The retrieval `BPLServlet` registers the management's `GetApplianceInfo` action so the page reads `mgmtURL` from its own origin. The build applies the default header blocks and the site's `template_changes.html` to the retrieval analyzer as well as the management pages, with asset paths valid from the viewer directory and menu targets at `mgmtURL`; marker checks alone do not establish that the site overlay reached the page. The input for plotting a PV is named for what it does (design, Page and header; decisions D1, D2, D6 of the design document).
- The Trend view uses the shared chart component in its simple form (one axis per unit, the legend and Chart options). Extend `createChart` only as needed to initialize, read and update its options, projection visibility and ranges by unit, notify the page of changes, and place controls and statistics in the approved composition. The Data view can show the existing statistics table independently of whether the Y projection is visible; Copy link and the existing viewer CSV remain available. Existing console callers keep their default layout and behavior. The viewer's one chart per unit becomes the split option of phase B.
- The address: `pv`, `from`, `to`, `tz`, `profile`, `view` (the selected view), and the existing chart options (`shape`, `yscale`, `marks`, `avg`, `band`, `window`, `yproj`, `yrange`), read on opening and written back as the page changes. Unknown names and unreadable values are ignored. Both Browse PVs routes (the plotted PVs and all selected PVs) carry the time window, time zone, display profile and applicable chart options; a copied analyzer link restores them after reopening, including ranges changed by pointer or keyboard and their reset. M27's display profiles remain available (design, Address; decisions of 2026-10-14 and D3 of the design document).
- The book: `interface.md`, `operating.md` and the data-viewer section of `retrieval.md`, including its figure, describe the page after this phase. Header-build explanations in `interface.md` and `building.md` match the retrieval overlay. `MgmtPageLinksTest` covers the page and the entry, and `SiteOverlayTest` covers the header application.

Out of scope: the gestures and options beyond the simple form, the Data table of samples and the full Export tab (M2, phase B); the analysis tabs and their per-tool input suitability checks (M3, M4, M5); the Browse PVs panels, which stay (D4 of the design).

##### Completion Criteria

- The analyzer opens from its menu entry and from both Browse PVs routes with the site header, logo, menu, time zone and display-profile choices above it; each menu item opens its console page; the Analyzer entry is marked; the address of the viewer opens on its own. Both a default build and a build with the shipped site template apply the expected header blocks, with working assets and menu targets in the packaged retrieval WAR (T1, T4, T6).
- No analyzer control uses the wording of archiving a PV.
- The page matches the composition and unavailable-tool presentation approved from the mockup. One common time-range control group serves all charts; unit-specific Y ranges are configured in Axes. The Trend view works, the existing statistics are available independently of projection visibility, and shared-chart changes preserve the console's simple form (T1, T2).
- The Browse PVs page with options set opens the analyzer with the same PVs, window, zone, profile and applicable options. Reopening Copy link restores this state, including each unit's range and projection visibility; changing or resetting a range updates the address. An address with `pv`, `from` and `to` alone opens as before; an unknown name or unreadable value is ignored (T2).
- A link between the management and retrieval origins carries the source's chosen time zone and display profile even when the destination has different stored preferences, including explicit `tz=browser` and `profile=default`. Valid explicit parameters take precedence. For a direct legacy link, a missing `tz` uses Browser regardless of a stored zone; a missing `profile` uses the retrieval origin's valid stored profile, or Default if none. Apply these rules independently when only one parameter is present. T5 checks this rule selected on 2026-11-04 (T5).
- The static check covers the analyzer page's marker blocks, the integration profile exercises the deployed analyzer and its retrieval BPL call, the default suite passes, and all affected book passages and the viewer figure describe the shipped page (T3, T4, T6). Local book building and link checking satisfy M1 documentation verification; published Pages verification belongs to M21 / Release Verification 7 by the decision of 2026-11-05.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- M59 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71): the chart component and its simple form, which the analyzer extends.
- M58 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71): the Browse PVs page and its control that opens the analyzer.
- D1: console pages.
- D2: the analyzer as a self-contained analysis tool built with the bundled D3.
- The decisions of M59 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) on the bundled D3, on the analyzer as a general signal analysis tool and on no external library bind this work as well.
- Decision Date 2026-10-15: the analyzer is an independent service, an application of its own that may be split out of the appliance in the long term, and it carries the same header as the console (logo, menu, time zone choice); the menu items are links that navigate to the console pages at the address of the console, and the analyzer is not shown in a frame of a console page (the owner chose this over the frame of 2026-10-14, which it replaces).
- Decision Date 2026-10-14, replaced on 2026-10-15 by the decision above: the analyzer is shown inside a console page in a frame (the console's content security policy gains `frame-src`; the retrieval web application sets no frame-blocking header), so that the site header, logo and menu are not copied into the retrieval web application; the viewer keeps working on its own address for its own window. The frame is sized by CSS to the window below the header, without `postMessage`. The two origins share only the address.
- Decision Date 2026-10-14: the options pass as one named parameter per option in the query string of the analyzer's address (`?`, where the viewer reads `pv`, `from` and `to` today), so that an address can be read and edited by hand; M1 lists the names.
- Decision Date 2026-10-14, superseded for `tz` and `profile` by the decision of 2026-11-04 below: Open in the analyzer passes the PVs, the time window and the options the user chose; an option left at its default is not passed and the analyzer applies its own default. New source-page and copied links now include both preference parameters, including their default choices; omission remains supported for direct legacy links.
- Decision Date 2026-10-14: Open in the analyzer carries the options chosen on the page as well as the PVs and the time window, so that the analyzer shows the picture the user was looking at; M1 gives the analyzer the parameters to read them.
- Decision Date 2026-10-15: the data viewer passes `strip: false` to the chart component, which draws a range strip above every chart otherwise. The placement question is settled by the shared time-range control decision of 2026-11-04 below.
- Decision Date 2026-10-15, superseded on 2026-10-19 by D4 of `docs/design-analyzer.md`: the Distribution, scatter plot and summary panels would leave the Browse PVs page when the analyzer carried them. The current decision keeps these panels on Browse PVs; their removal is not M1 work.
- Decision Date 2026-10-15: the control of the Browse PVs page, which M58 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) builds with the PVs and the time window, gets the options added by this item, so the parameters are named where they are read.
- Decision Date 2026-10-14: the analyzer reads, from its address, the PVs, the time window and the options of the simple form (line, Y scale, sample marks, running average, standard deviation band and its window, the Y projection, and the range of each axis), so that the control into the analyzer of the other pages opens the same picture; an option the user did not set is not in the address and the analyzer applies its own default; each option is one named parameter in the query string, a name it does not know or a value it cannot read is ignored so that an address with `pv`, `from` and `to` alone opens as before, and the range of an axis is given by the unit of the axis (`none` for a PV with no unit); the names and form of the parameters are settled in this milestone.
- D5 (Decision Date 2026-10-19): the analyzer is built in five phases; this row is phase A, planned from `docs/design-analyzer.md`, whose decisions D1 to D6 of 2026-10-19 bind it (the independent service with the console's menu, `getApplianceInfo` on the retrieval, the defaults of the options, the Browse PVs panels staying, the time axis as drawn, the menu that navigates to the other side).
- M67 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) step 5 (the header of the analyzer with the time zone box and the hand-over of `tz`) is done here.
- The pre-implementation header path applied the site template and default synchronization only to management pages. M1 includes the retrieval analyzer in both build paths and rebases its shared assets; T6 verifies the deployed default and site headers.
- The pre-implementation chart kept controls and projection in the widget and coupled statistics to projection. M1 exposes copied state, change callbacks and external placement, including independent statistics, while retaining the console defaults; T1 and T2 verify these paths.
- Observed 2026-11-04 22:03-22:05 UTC (T5 baseline): the packaged viewer honors an explicit valid `profile`; an omitted profile uses retrieval-origin storage, so a source Default choice opened as Monochrome in all four tested routes. The viewer's time inputs, axes and tooltip use the browser zone even with `tz=UTC`; the existing viewer does not apply `currentZone()` to these displays. Only Open all selected supplied non-default `tz`; the plotted-PV, enlarged-chart and menu routes omitted it. A viewer range change removed both preference parameters. Direct absent, explicit, default and invalid parameters were observed. Evidence and reproducible procedure: `work/analyzer-m1-baseline/report.md`, `run.py`, `observe.cjs` and `run-002/`; the packaged JavaScript matched the current source. This baseline establishes existing behavior, not the final M1 URL rule or T5 completion.
- Observed 2026-11-04: the existing mockup, selected through its M1 scope control, retained Trend and explained an unavailable tab activated by keyboard. At 1440, 720 and 320 px it had no page-wide horizontal overflow; settings were beside the plot at 1440 px and below it at the narrower widths. Evidence: `work/analyzer-m1-baseline/mockup-observation/observations.json` and its screenshots, produced by `observe-mockup.cjs`. This is preview evidence only; the later composition decision is recorded below, and the packaged-product checks have their own Verification Results.
- Decision Date 2026-11-04: record the input suitability checks in the analyzer plans now, and implement and verify them when each analysis tab or tool is developed in M3, M4 or M5. These checks are not an additional M1 completion criterion or prerequisite. The scheduling decision does not accept the remaining plans or authorize product implementation.
- Decision Date 2026-11-04: select option 1 for direct legacy links, preserving the observed Browser time zone and stored display profile. Valid explicit `tz` and `profile` win independently; missing `tz` uses Browser even if a zone is stored, and missing `profile` uses the retrieval origin's valid stored profile or Default. Unreadable values remain ignored under the existing M1 rule. New source-page links, including the menu, and Copy link explicitly carry both effective preferences, including `tz=browser` and `profile=default`; range changes retain them. This settles the preference fallback choice without accepting the complete M1 implementation plan or authorizing product implementation.
- Decision Date 2026-11-04: use one page-level time-range control group with the common selection above the analysis tabs, rather than repeating it for each chart. All charts use the common time range; Axes owns each unit's Y-range settings. This placement applies to the analyzer; existing console chart placement remains unchanged. It does not accept the complete implementation plan or authorize product implementation.
- Decision Date 2026-11-04: accept the displayed M1 foundation composition, with the right-hand settings panel moving below the plot at compact widths, statistics below the plot and seven unavailable tabs that retain Trend and explain their status. Authorize implementation of the current five-step M1 plan following the completed preference and shared-range choices. M2-M5 capabilities and open numerical decisions remain outside this authorization.

- Decision Date 2026-11-05: extract an independent reusable canvas for time and numeric axes from the existing Browse PVs chart and apply it to the offline mockup. The production Trend retains its existing controls; optional X keyboard, Ctrl+wheel and navigation controls are exercised by the mockup. This authorizes the shared module and its consumers, not the remaining M2-M5 product capabilities.
- Decision Date 2026-11-05: add shared hover support for the mockup plots and preserve the page position when a range change redraws the canvas. The mockup retains the original summary and adds statistics for the visible range, uses information buttons for detailed help, and adds histogram bounds and a custom bin count alongside the existing presets. These are reusable canvas and preview improvements; the production analysis tabs remain M2-M5 work.

- Decision Date 2026-11-05: complete M1 with its passed implementation, package, browser, integration, local documentation and Maven CI checks. Move Pages deployment verification from M1 / T4 to M21 / Release Verification 7 after the release merge into master. The deployment check remains required for the release and is not reported as performed by M1. Issue #39 remains open because it covers M1-M5.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: accepted
Plan Acceptance: 2026-11-04; the M1 foundation composition and current five-step plan after the preference and shared-range decisions. Independent plan review on 2026-11-05 UTC found no additional requirement. 2026-11-05; accept the final verification scope with Pages deployment assigned to the release.
Implementation Authorization: 2026-11-04; explicit instruction to proceed with the current M1 plan after the final placement choice. 2026-11-05; explicit instructions to make the canvas independently reusable, apply it to the mockup, extend hover to its other plots and preserve the page position during redraws. The mockup summary, help and histogram controls follow the same dated requests. 2026-11-05; explicit instruction to record M1 completion and the release timing of Pages verification.
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

1. Implement the accepted M1 foundation in `docs/design-analyzer-ui.md`, demonstrated by `work/analyzer-mockup/index.html`; retain its configuration panels and unavailable-tool presentation. Apply the shared time-range control placement selected on 2026-11-04: one group with the common selection above the tabs, with per-unit Y ranges in Axes. Apply the preference rule selected from the T5 baseline on 2026-11-04: a missing `tz` uses Browser, a missing `profile` uses the stored profile or Default, and valid explicit values win independently. New source-page and copied links explicitly carry both effective preferences, including Browser and Default. Plan acceptance and implementation authorization remain separate.
2. Register `GetApplianceInfo` in the retrieval `BPLServlet`. Give `archViewer.html` the approved header, menu, time-zone and profile controls and the renamed PV input, using `mgmtURL` for console navigation; keep `analyzer-forward.js` forwarding the query string. Extend the default header synchronization and `SiteOverlay` build paths to the retrieval analyzer, resolving shared assets relative to the viewer directory and applying the site's blocks once. Extend the static marker checks and `SiteOverlayTest`, and check the packaged default and site-overlaid WARs. Checks: T1, T4, T6.
3. Extend `chart/widget.js`, `chart/draw.js`, `chart/gestures.js` and `chart/strip.js` with initialization, state access and change notifications for options, projection and ranges by unit. Allow the analyzer to place controls and statistics in the approved layout without reaching into private DOM or maps; retain default placement for existing console callers. Separate statistics output from projection visibility and notify the page after pointer, keyboard and reset actions. Extract axes, clipping and input geometry into chart/canvas.js, with caller-owned domains and data layers; reuse it in drawTimeSeries and the offline numeric plots as specified in docs/design-chart-canvas.md. Provide optional shared hover and preserve viewport position during redraws, including focus restoration on a value axis. Checks: T1, T2.
4. Build the approved composition in `archViewer.html`, `viewer.js` and `viewer.css` around `createChart`, with the working Trend view, existing statistics, Copy link and viewer CSV. Place the time-range controls once with the common selection above the tabs and the unit-specific Y-range controls in Axes. In `viewer.js` and the two analyzer-link paths in `browse.js`, read, validate and write the M1 parameters, including `profile`, `yproj` and repeated `yrange`; preserve M27's profile selector and line combinations. Apply the accepted time-zone/profile precedence rule, and restore state when reopening an address. Check both the chart link, including its enlarged view, and Open all selected in the analyzer. Checks: T1, T2, T5.
5. Update `interface.md`, `operating.md`, the data-viewer section and figure in `retrieval.md`, and the header-overlay description in `building.md`; update the M1 portions of `docs/design-analyzer.md` to the accepted composition and URL contract. Extend `MgmtPageLinksTest` to request the retrieval analyzer, its assets and its own `getApplianceInfo` action, with real browser checks for links built by JavaScript. Run the book build and link check, the static checks, the explicit integration test and the default suite. Checks: T3, T4, T6.

#### M2 - Analyzer phase B: interaction, options and export

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M11
Identity History: M11 to M2 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #39 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/39)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

Phase B of `docs/design-analyzer.md` (D5): extend the reusable chart canvas with the applicable functions already implemented by bundled D3, connect them using established interaction conventions, and complete the Data and Export tabs on the page of phase A. The preparation direction was confirmed on 2026-11-05; the detailed plan remains a draft.

##### Scope

- Gestures: a toolbar with Select initially active and Pan or Box zoom as alternatives, independent wheel and hover controls, pointer-centered wheel and pinch zoom within limits, range history and reset, mouse and touch selection, legend visibility, series ordering, pinned samples and two reference cursors with time/value differences. Use the D3 behaviors and the reference mapping below; commit a changed retrieval range only after a gesture ends.
- Options (design, Reference tools, Options the tools offer): line width and style, a gap threshold that disconnects the line, a fill between two signals, soft and fixed axis ranges typed per unit, grid lines, centered zero, SI prefixes, an axis label, the averaging interval in seconds or raw samples forced, the min-max band from the retrieval no-fill `minSample` and `maxSample` operators with the mean, a split by unit (one plot per unit sharing the time axis), the legend in table mode with computed values, the tooltip in single or all mode with a sort order, decimals and a display name per signal.
- The Data tab: the table of samples in view with their time, value, severity, status and retrieval provenance; preserve source seconds and nanoseconds independently of drawing coordinates. Keep the selected-data Summary and the changing Visible range statistics distinct, following the existing mockup contract.
- The Export tab (design, Export): the range (shown or typed), the source (the samples shown, the raw samples, N bins with minimum, mean and maximum, a linear interpolation), the format (wide or long CSV, severity and status, digits, ISO 8601 in the zone or UNIX milliseconds, the unit in the header), the products (data CSV, statistics CSV, plot as PNG or SVG, the address copied), and the retrieval's own `getData.csv` and `getData.mat` addresses for large ranges.
- The address names of every new option, read and written as in phase A; the book.

The display defaults are fixed by `design-analyzer.md` D12-D14: Select initially active; optional line-end names off; one sample window for the running average and standard deviation band, default 30. The Accepted Defaults table below defines their address behavior.

Out of scope: implementation of the analysis tabs (M3, M4, M5); new Browse PVs controls; a D3 version upgrade or another chart library; transitions on range changes, deferred past M5 by the plan review of 2026-11-06 because they add an address parameter and T1/T4 cases without an analysis function. Shared-module changes must preserve the existing Browse PVs behavior. The later-phase D3 inventory below records reuse opportunities without moving those tools into M2.

##### Completion Criteria

- Each gesture and option is observed on archived fixture data with mouse, keyboard and touch. A changed retrieval range issues one request batch after the gesture; selection, hover, Y-only changes and programmatic synchronization do not issue duplicate requests.
- The Data tab preserves the retrieved sample metadata. Summary retains its selected dataset; Visible range is calculated from that dataset inside the displayed axes and identifies its source and interval. A display change cannot silently turn statistics of averages into statistics of raw samples.
- Export produces each product of the Scope. Optimized export reports the requested bin count, effective interval and actual returned bin count with minimum, mean and maximum; values equal the retrieval's for the same range and the no-fill operators `meanSample_N`, `minSample_N` and `maxSample_N`. Drawing precision and number formatting do not overwrite source values or timestamps.
- Every option is in the address, and an address with the options of phase A alone opens as before.
- The default suite passes and the book describes the controls.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- M1: the page, the drawer and the address of phase A.
- D2: the analyzer as a self-contained analysis tool built with the bundled D3.
- D5 (Decision Date 2026-10-19): phase B of the analyzer, planned from `docs/design-analyzer.md`.
- Decision Date 2026-11-05: prepare the plan by reusing applicable bundled D3 functions and matching established reference behavior for the application connections (`design-analyzer.md`, D11). This confirms the preparation direction, not complete-plan acceptance or implementation authorization.
- Decision Date 2026-11-05: accept Select as the initial drag tool, optional line-end names off by default, and one shared sample window for the running average and standard deviation band, retaining default 30 and choices 10, 30 and 60 (`design-analyzer.md`, D12-D14). These choices are resolved. The M2 Address Contract defines the remaining option names, defaults and validation rules; complete-plan acceptance and implementation authorization remain separate.
- Decision Date 2026-11-06: accept the complete M2 plan as reviewed, with pins kept in page state across navigation, cursors reading the last observation at or before their time, the no-fill `meanSample`, `minSample` and `maxSample` operators throughout, the preceding observation of an interpolated export taken from `data/getDataAtTime`, and transitions on range changes deferred past M5; authorize its implementation.
- Decision Date 2026-11-06: the limits of a navigation gesture on the time axis (wheel, pinch) are a window of at least one second and at most ten years; a range typed on the page is not bound by them. Reset and the history stay available on a window without samples.
- Decision Date 2026-11-06: the address follows the standards of its parts instead of its own key syntax: OpenAPI 3 parameter styles (`form` lists, `deepObject` per-target settings), RFC 3339 times, ISO 8601 durations, `true`/`false` booleans, camelCase names as the appliance API; the parameters are declared in `docs/analyzer-address.openapi.json`. The call needs `pv` alone, as pvinfo writes it, and may add `from` and `to`; the page writes the rest. The parameters are the settings the page already offers, and none is added. The M2 Address Contract of this register is revised accordingly.

##### D3 Reuse And Reference Behavior

The bundled `staticcontent/js/vendor/d3.min.js` is the implementation baseline. On 2026-11-05, loading that actual bundle with Node reported version 7.9.0, 578 public names, and all API names in the inventory below present. This is API availability evidence, not browser-interaction verification. The [D3 API index](https://d3js.org/api), [zoom](https://d3js.org/d3-zoom), [brush](https://d3js.org/d3-brush) and [drag](https://d3js.org/d3-drag) documentation define the primitives; implementation must remain compatible with the bundled version.

| Capability | Reuse from D3 | Application connection | Phase |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Pan, wheel, pinch, bounded navigation | `zoom`, `zoomIdentity`, `zoomTransform`; extent, filter and transform APIs | Axis targeting, limits, active-tool routing and completed-range notification | M2 |
| Rectangular and axis selection | `brush`, `brushX`, `brushY`, `brushSelection` | Distinguish a selected region from a zoomed domain; map selection to the caller's axes | M2 |
| Reference cursors and pins | `drag`, `pointer`, `pointers`, `dispatch` | Sample identity, two-cursor differences, pin lifetime and keyboard equivalents | M2 |
| Scales, axes and readable values | `scaleLinear`, `scaleLog`, `scaleSymlog`, `scaleSqrt`, `scaleTime`, `scaleUtc`, `axisBottom`, `axisLeft`, `format`, `formatPrefix`, `timeFormat`, `utcFormat` | Per-unit axes, validity checks, soft/fixed limits, timezone and existing tick placement | M2 |
| Lines, bands and updates | `line`, `area`, `curveStepAfter`, `curveLinear`, `curveMonotoneX` | Gap policy, fill pairing, min/max retrieval and clipping | M2 |
| Actual-sample hover | `bisector` for sorted time samples; `Delaunay` or `quadtree` for spatial points | Shared tooltip content, units, hidden-series policy and display-space distance | M2; spatial consumers in M4-M5 |
| Summary and grouping | `min`, `max`, `extent`, `count`, `mean`, `median`, `variance`, `deviation`, `quantile`, `group`, `rollup` | Input dataset identity, empty/single-sample rules and separate Summary/Visible range | M2 foundation; additional statistics in M3 |
| CSV/TSV encoding | `csvFormat`, `csvFormatRows`, `tsvFormat` | Products, source selection, timestamp precision, metadata, download and server-streaming links | M2 CSV; TSV only if later included in scope |
| Histograms | `bin`, `thresholdSturges`, `thresholdScott`, `thresholdFreedmanDiaconis` | Existing custom-count and bin-range contract, included/excluded counts and fit inputs | M3 |
| Density, regions and matrix ordering | `contours`, `contourDensity`, `polygonContains`, `hierarchy`, `cluster` | Analysis results, region meaning and clustering calculations not supplied by drawing APIs | M4-M5 |
| Reproducible numerical fixtures | `randomLcg`, `randomNormal` and applicable bundled random generators | Known distributions and independent expected results; retain the shipped fixture datasets | M3-M5 |

Use the full applicable D3 families already inventoried in `design-analyzer.md`, rather than reproducing their algorithms. Availability alone does not add an unrelated visualization to the product. Native browser facilities still own HTTP cancellation, clipboard, SVG serialization and image download; D3 does not provide the Analyzer's toolbar, history, retrieval policy, FFT or fitting solver.

The reference sources were read on 2026-11-05. [Bokeh plot tools](https://docs.bokeh.org/en/latest/docs/user_guide/interaction/tools.html) supplies one active tool per gesture category, selection distinct from box zoom, passive inspectors, history and original-range reset. [Grafana Time series](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/latest/visualizations/panels-visualizations/visualizations/time-series/) supplies display controls, soft/fixed limits and tooltip/legend conventions. [Phoebus Data Browser export](https://control-system-studio.readthedocs.io/en/latest/app/databrowser/doc/#exporting-data) supplies independently chosen export range and source, including plotted, raw, optimized and interpolated data. These tools are behavioral references; their libraries are not dependencies.

The following is the proposed Analyzer contract, including explicit adaptations for archived data. It is not a claim that every reference implements these exact combinations.

| Control or event | Proposed observable behavior | D3 and application responsibilities |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Drag toolbar | `Select`, `Pan` and `Box zoom` are mutually exclusive; Select is initially active and the active button is visible. Wheel and hover remain independent; existing console defaults remain unchanged. | Route event ownership through D3 filters and distinct overlay targets; a cursor handle wins over plot navigation. Do not attach competing handlers to the same gesture. |
| Select | Highlight a candidate time region using `brushX` without changing axes, Summary or retrieval. Moving/resizing its handles updates that region. An explicit Apply uses the existing common time selection; Escape clears it. | Preserve D3 brush modifiers where applicable. This is a single candidate range, not multi-selection or an implicit analysis filter. |
| Pan and Box zoom | Pan moves the displayed domain; Box zoom commits the drawn rectangle. Trend defaults to X-only navigation; a Y or XY target names a specific unit axis, including in a split plot. | `zoom` handles pan; `brushX`, `brushY` or `brush` supplies the zoom extent. Derive domains from stable base scales; retain independent Y ranges and synchronize only the shared X range. |
| Wheel and touch | When the wheel option is enabled, Ctrl+wheel zooms around the pointer, as the existing canvas does; a wheel without Ctrl never zooms, so page scrolling stays available everywhere. Pinch zooms around the touch center. One-finger input follows the active tool; two fingers navigate. | Use D3 delta normalization, touch handling and extent controls. Route a second touch away from a pending brush without committing it. Do not invent a conflicting Shift-to-pan shortcut. |
| Undo, Redo and Reset | Each completed navigation or axis-setting action records the X range and the complete state of every affected Y axis: stable axis ID, scale kind, auto/soft/fixed policy, optional soft bounds, fixed bounds and displayed domain. Undo/Redo restores the policy as well as the numbers; a new action after Undo discards Redo. Reset restores the current selected-data range and initial Y settings. | Y navigation establishes a fixed range for the targeted axis; Undo restores the preceding automatic or soft behavior. Automatic/soft axes recompute from the current display data after restoration and subsequent retrieval; fixed axes retain their bounds. Keep double-click reset as an explicit compatibility adaptation, disable D3's competing double-click zoom, and ignore no-op/cancelled actions. |
| Hover, pins and cursors | Read actual observations, showing units and source resolution. Hidden series do not supply hover candidates. Pins retain sample identity and keep their observation in page state across navigation and display-resolution changes; two movable cursors show, per visible series, the last observation at or before the cursor time with that observation's timestamp, show time/value differences, and do not invent values across gaps. | D3 performs pointer mapping and lookup; shared hover renders text. Page code owns sample semantics. Suppress hover during drag; clear invalid pins/cursors when the input dataset changes. |
| Display options | Line style, gaps, fill, grid, per-unit soft/fixed ranges, zero centering, numeric format, legend table and tooltip modes have consistent controls and apply without modifying samples. | D3 supplies geometry/scales/formatting. The caller validates scale domains and pairs signals for fill; unsupported combinations explain the reason through the existing information card. |
| Selection and display state | Keep the selected dataset and its Summary separate from the display domain. Visible range filters the selected dataset using displayed axes; changing only display resolution does not replace that dataset. | Label raw versus averaged source and interval for both statistics and plotted data. Explicit PV/time/source changes establish a new dataset and clear incompatible history. Later tools preserve fit inputs and histogram bin settings on display-only changes. |
| Retrieval completion | During navigation, redraw existing data. After a completed X-range change, start at most one batch for that final range and the required operators; cache hits need no HTTP request. Y-only changes, hover and selection alone issue none. | A batch may include mean, min and max requests per PV. Keep AbortController and generation checks; D3 programmatic events must not start feedback requests. Keep the original Summary dataset separate from any replacement display data. |
| Export | Choose shown or typed range independently, then Plot, Raw, Optimized or Interpolated source. Raw is the proposed initial source, following Phoebus. Exported metadata identifies the effective range, source and interval. Interpolated output follows the contract below. | Use D3 CSV escaping, preserve original `secs`/`nanos` and status, and derive formatted columns separately. Optimized N is a requested count: retrieval suffixes are integer seconds, so show the effective interval and actual count. Large exports use the existing endpoints only for products those endpoints actually provide. |
| Reuse and accessibility | The same controls operate on time and numeric canvases. Keyboard alternatives, focus, page scroll position and per-instance isolation survive redraw, resize and disposal. Keep one common PV/time selector above the tabs. | The canvas has no PV, HTTP, storage or analysis dependency. The caller supplies domains and callbacks. Adapt D3 listener cleanup and interrupted gestures explicitly; replacing SVG nodes alone is not a sufficient lifecycle contract. |

##### Accepted Defaults

| Setting | Behavior | Address |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Drag tool | Select initially; Pan and Box zoom are alternatives. Select only marks a candidate range until Apply. | `drag=select`, `drag=pan` or `drag=box`; missing or invalid values use Select. Omit the default when writing. |
| Line-end names | Off initially. When enabled, identify visible series at their last visible finite observation; hidden series and series without such an observation have no label. Keep the legend independent. | `endLabels=true` enables names; other or missing values mean off. Write only the enabled value. |
| Running-statistics window | One window for the running average and standard deviation band: 10, 30 or 60 samples, default 30. Their enable controls remain independent. This sample count does not control retrieval averaging or the min-max band. | Preserve the existing `window` parameter; missing or invalid values use 30, and the writer omits 30. Add no separate average or band window parameter. |

These defaults are M2 requirements, not a claim that its new controls already exist in the shipped Analyzer.

##### M2 Address Contract

The address of the Analyzer is its calling interface: another tool opens the page with it, and the page writes its state back into it so that the same view opens again. The parameters, their serialization and their schemas are declared in `docs/analyzer-address.openapi.json` (OpenAPI 3.0.3); this section states the semantics the declaration cannot. The declaration follows the standards the pieces have: RFC 3986 and the WHATWG URL Standard for the query (`URLSearchParams` once for reading and once for writing), OpenAPI parameter styles for lists (`form`, a repeated parameter or a comma-separated value) and per-target settings (`deepObject`, `width[PV]=3`, `axisMin[mA]=0`, `export[source]=raw`), RFC 3339 in UTC for a time, ISO 8601 for a duration (`PT5S`, `PT1.5S`), `true` and `false` for a boolean. Names are camelCase as the appliance's own API writes them (`searchPeriod`, `fetchLatestMetadata`). `chart/state.js` owns reading and writing; the viewer owns PV identity, retrieval and export; the canvas receives validated values and has no URL dependency. The parameters are the settings the page already offers; the contract adds none.

**The call.** A caller needs `pv` alone, repeated once per PV in series order, as pvinfo writes it (`archViewer.html?pv=A&pv=B`); it may add `from` and `to`. Everything else has a default, so a short call opens a complete view, and the page writes the full address back for Copy link.

| Parameter | Value | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `pv` (repeated) | PV names; the first occurrence of a name keeps its place | The series and their order; per-PV settings stay bound to the name |
| `from`, `to` | RFC 3339 UTC, an ordered pair | The displayed range; one invalid or missing, the last hour ending now (M1) |

**Selection and display.** `selectedFrom` and `selectedTo` keep the selected range of the Summary and of Reset when navigation moved the display; absent, partial or unordered, the displayed range is used. `sample` is the resolution of the selected dataset (`raw`, or a whole number of seconds from 1 through 2147483647 as an ISO 8601 duration, `PT5S` or `PT5M`), resolved once when a selection is made and written with it; `interval` is the display resolution (`raw` or such a duration; absent, about 700 means over the displayed range). `minMax` adds the min-max band of the display means. `split=unit` draws one panel per unit. `hide` (repeated) names the hidden PVs, which keep their data and Summary rows. Navigation changes `from`, `to` and the display retrieval only; an explicit PV, selected-time or source change establishes a new selected dataset, clears incompatible history and references, and rewrites `sample`.

**Tools and references.** `drag` (`select`, the default, `pan`, `box`), `wheel` (default `false`), `hover` (default `true`), `nav` (`x`, the default, `y`, `xy`) with `navUnit` (required for `y` and `xy`; absent or unknown, `nav` falls back to `x`; with `nav=x` it is ignored and not written). `cursor1` and `cursor2` are RFC 3339 times with up to nine fractional digits; the page keeps the digits it reads and writes them back unchanged while the cursor has not moved. `pin` (repeated) is `PV@time`, the time of the pinned observation with its nanoseconds; the page restores the value from the observation of the display dataset at exactly that time (the display request is the `interval`, `from` and `to` of the same address) and lists a pin without one as unavailable, without inventing or fetching a substitute. Pins keep their observation in page state across navigation and resolution changes, as the interaction contract says; dataset changes clear them.

**Display options.** `legend` (`list`, the default, `table`, `off`), `legendStats` (comma-separated, in order, from `min`, `max`, `mean`, `median`, `std`, `last`, `count`; default `min,max,mean,last`; an entirely invalid list falls back to the default), `tooltip` (`all`, the default, `single`), `tooltipSort` (`series`, the default, `asc`, `desc`), `grid` (`off`, `x`, `y`, the default, `both`), `endLabels` (default `false`). Per PV, keyed by the name: `width[PV]` (0.5 to 10), `dash[PV]` (`solid`, `dash`, `dot`, `dashdot`), `gap[PV]` (a duration), `fill[PV]` (another selected PV of the same unit; a self pair or another unit is dropped), `decimals[PV]` (0 to 15), `name[PV]` (plain text of at most 128 code points). A setting, a `hide` entry, a `fill` target or a `pin` that names a PV not in `pv` is dropped.

**Axes.** Per unit, keyed by the exact unit string, the empty key for no unit (`axisLabel[]=Value`): `axisScale[UNIT]` (`linear`, `symlog`, `log`, `sqrt`; absent, the chart's `yscale`), `axisSymlog[UNIT]` (a positive constant, kept while another scale is chosen), `axisMin[UNIT]` with `axisMax[UNIT]` (a fixed range, policy fixed; one without the other, or min not below max, drops the pair), `axisSoftMin[UNIT]` and `axisSoftMax[UNIT]` (soft bounds the automatic range widens to, policy soft; either may be given, both only with min below max, else the pair is dropped), `axisZero[UNIT]` (zero centered on a linear or symmetric log automatic or soft axis), `axisPrefix[UNIT]=si`, `axisLabel[UNIT]`. The policy of a unit follows from the bounds present: fixed with a fixed pair, soft with a soft bound, auto otherwise; a fixed pair wins over soft bounds. Fixed bounds must suit the scale (positive for log, nonnegative for sqrt); bounds that do not are dropped and the automatic domain is shown with the scale note. The legacy `yrange=unit:low:high` (M1, `none` for no unit, the last valid entry of a unit winning) is read as a fixed pair for a unit without `axisMin` and `axisMax`, and is not written. Writing emits the bounds of the policy in force and omits the others; a computed automatic domain is never written as fixed. Navigating Y establishes a fixed pair for the targeted unit, and Undo restores the policy and bounds before the address is written.

**Export.** `export[...]` restores the choices of the Export tab and never runs a product: `product` (`data`, the default, `statistics`, `png`, `svg`, `link`, `serverCsv`, `serverMat`), `range` (`view`, the default, `custom`), `from` and `to` (RFC 3339; a custom range needs both, ordered, else the shown range is used with a note), `source` (`plot`, `raw`, the default, `optimized`, `interpolated`), `bins` (1 to 1000000, default 700), `step` (a duration, default `PT1S`), `gap` (a duration, default none), `layout` (`long`, the default, `wide`), `alarms` (default `true`), `values` (`source`, the default, `fixed`, `exponential`), `decimals` (0 to 15, default 6), `time` (`iso`, the default, `unixms`), `units` (default `false`), `stats` (`visible`, the default, `selected`). An invalid field falls back to its default; the other fields stay. A server product with a source or layout the server does not produce keeps the choices, disables its action and says why.

**Phase A.** `shape`, `yscale`, `marks`, `avg`, `band`, `window`, `yproj`, `tz`, `profile` and `view` keep their M1 names, values and defaults, and `tz` and `profile` are written as M1 writes them.

**Reading.** The brackets of a deepObject key and of a PV name are percent-encoded in the address the page writes, as `URLSearchParams` does; a caller may leave them raw (`encodeURI` does), because the appliance's connector accepts a raw bracket (`relaxedQueryChars="[]"`, set by `scripts/run-local-appliance.bash` and the installation steps of the book), while a container without that setting answers 400. A single parameter takes its first occurrence; a repeated parameter keeps the first occurrence of each valid distinct item in order. A number is a nonempty finite decimal, an integer base-10 digits in range, a boolean `true` or `false`; a value that is not so falls back to the default, and an invalid item of a list or a per-target setting is dropped. A pair (`from`/`to`, `selectedFrom`/`selectedTo`, a fixed range, a custom export range) is applied whole or not at all. Unknown parameters are ignored and not written back. Per-unit settings wait for the metadata of the PVs and are never attached to another unit. The page restores the whole state before it draws or retrieves, issues no retrieval for pins or cursors, and starts no export.

**Writing.** The page writes `pv` first in series order, then every other parameter in code-point order of its name, so that one state has one address and a second write equals the first. It writes `from` and `to` always, `tz` and `profile` as M1 does, a resolved `sample` once a dataset is established, `selectedFrom` and `selectedTo` only when they differ from `from` and `to`, and every other parameter only when it differs from its default or carries a valid per-target override. It omits a setting of a PV no longer selected and the unknown parameters it read. In-page changes use `replaceState`; opening an address starts one new baseline without a history.

**T4 coverage.** The real packaged reader and writer exercise every parameter above: for a scalar absent, valid, invalid and duplicate values; for a list order, duplicates, invalid items and removal; and these cases:

| Case | Required result |
| --- | --- |
| A pvinfo-style call (`pv` only, one and several) and an M1 address with `yrange`, `tz` and `profile` | The last hour opens; the legacy range, zone and profile apply |
| Selected range and resolution differ from the displayed ones | Summary and Reset keep the selected dataset after a reload; display navigation never rewrites `sample` |
| PV names with `:`, `+`, brackets, slash or `#`; unit labels with spaces, slash or `%`; the empty unit | One encoding pass; each setting stays attached to its full key |
| Series reorder, hide, removal and later metadata arrival | Settings follow the name and the unit, never a list index |
| Fixed, soft and automatic policies, partial bounds, zero, log or sqrt with unsuitable bounds, a symlog constant | Pairs applied whole or dropped, the policy follows the bounds, no automatic domain written as fixed |
| Pins at exact nanoseconds and at a time without an observation; cursors with nine fractional digits | The value is restored or the pin is unavailable; the digits round-trip unchanged |
| The export choices, a custom range, an invalid step or gap, an unavailable server combination | The form is restored or falls back as stated; nothing is exported on load |
| Copy, reload, Back and Forward, a second write | The same normalized address; one request batch; no inherited history |

##### History Restoration Contract

The displayed domain is a result, not a substitute for the axis policy. An automatic axis has no fixed bounds; a soft axis has optional minimum/maximum suggestions and still expands to include its current data; a fixed axis retains explicitly chosen bounds. Preserve absent bounds as absent when copying history or writing an address. A snapshot also retains the scale configuration needed to interpret the domain, including the symlog constant when applicable. Restore the policy and scale configuration before recomputing the domain, then synchronize D3 without recording another history action or starting another request batch.

History belongs to one selected dataset and axis identity set. A change of PVs, selected time range, source or unit identity establishes a new baseline and clears incompatible entries. A display-resolution response alone does not clear history or change an automatic/soft policy into fixed. Validate restored domains through the same scale-validity and navigation-limit checks as pointer and typed input; a raw `zoom.transform` call does not enforce D3 scale/translation extents. Reset is one undoable action when it changes state; Undo/Redo do not themselves append entries. Navigation history does not change the active drag tool.

##### Interpolated Export Contract

This is a page calculation over raw observations, independent of the plotted averaging interval. The retrieval `linear_N` collector evaluates a bin midpoint and can return a bin's first value; it is not the specification for this product. A server CSV/MAT link must not be labelled as the page's Interpolated product unless it implements this contract.

- In this contract `from` and `to` are the effective export range: the displayed range for `export[range]=view`, or `export[from]`/`export[to]` for `custom`. The export interval is a positive duration in seconds, exactly representable in integer nanoseconds; reject non-finite, non-positive or over-precision input. Grid timestamps are `from + k * interval` for integer `k >= 0` while the timestamp is at or before `to`. Include `to` only when it lies on that grid; do not append a shorter final interval. Compare source timestamps as `(secs, nanos)` or integer nanoseconds, not floating-point epoch nanoseconds or rounded JavaScript Dates.
- A finite original observation exactly on a grid timestamp keeps its value, timestamp, severity and status and is marked `observed`. For a time strictly between two adjacent, finite observations with distinct timestamps, use `d3.bisector` to locate the neighbors and `d3.interpolateNumber(leftValue, rightValue)(fraction)` with `fraction = (time - leftTime) / (rightTime - leftTime)`. Compute differences before converting the fraction to floating point. Mark the generated value `interpolated`; reject a non-finite result.
- Do not extrapolate before the first or after the last available observation. The observation that precedes `from` is available from the server: before calculating, request the retrieval's `data/getDataAtTime` once per export (the selected PV names as a JSON array in the POST body; `at=from` and `searchPeriod=P1D`, the server default sent explicitly, as query parameters; no address parameter carries them); it returns, for each PV, the last stored observation at or before `from` within that period and the 31 days the server adds, with `secs`, `nanos`, `val`, `severity` and `status`. That observation is the left neighbor of the grid timestamps that precede the first raw observation of the range; it is not a row of Data or of the export. When the raw range already holds an observation at exactly `from`, that raw observation is used and the preceding observation is discarded without comparison. The `searchPeriod` is part of the export metadata. No endpoint supplies the observation that follows `to`, so grid timestamps after the last observation at or before `to` are `no_bracket`; a PV whose preceding observation lies outside the search period is likewise `no_bracket` before its first observation. Do not interpolate across an explicit missing/invalid observation, a recorded disconnect/restart interval or an endpoint with invalid/unknown alarm severity. Keep these breaks while locating neighbors; filtering invalid records out first must not join the surrounding observations. A long interval between normal event-driven archive samples alone is not proof of a disconnection. An explicit maximum interpolation gap may further restrict neighbor distance; when unset, no duration-only cutoff is inferred. This setting is independent of the plot's display-gap threshold and is included in export metadata and the copied address.
- Exact duplicate records at one timestamp may collapse only when value, severity, status and gap metadata agree. Conflicting records at one timestamp produce a missing grid result at that instant and break interpolation across that instant. Do not silently choose the first or last conflicting value.
- Keep a row at every grid timestamp. An unavailable result has an empty value and empty severity/status, `quality=missing`, and a reason such as `no_bracket`, `gap`, `invalid_input` or `conflicting_timestamp`. A generated value has `quality=interpolated`; when alarm columns are requested, its severity is the maximum of the two valid endpoint severities, its status is empty because no IOC reported that calculated value, and separate left/right severity/status columns retain the inputs. Exact observations use `quality=observed` and their original alarm fields, even when the reported observation has an alarm. Quality and missing-reason columns remain present when optional alarm columns are disabled; do not invent an EPICS status code for a calculated value.
- Long and wide exports use the same grid and per-signal calculation. Wide format leaves a missing signal's cell empty without shifting any other signal to a different row. ISO 8601 and UNIX-millisecond output are exact textual representations derived from the integer timestamp; UNIX milliseconds may carry fractional digits. Explicit value formatting happens after calculation and does not change the stored observations or comparison inputs.

T3 checks this calculation against an independent high-precision arithmetic oracle built from the raw JSON actually returned by retrieval. Use exact equality for timestamp pairs, grid membership, row counts, quality, missing reasons and alarm fields. Before display rounding, require each generated value to satisfy `abs(actual - expected) <= 1e-12 * max(1, abs(leftValue), abs(rightValue))`; exact observations retain the decoded source value without a tolerance. The oracle is a comparison only and never replaces an internal span of the page's execution path.

| Case | Required result |
| --- | --- |
| Grid meets an original observation | Original value and alarm fields; `observed` |
| Grid falls between two eligible observations | Linear interpolation within the stated tolerance; derived quality and endpoint alarm fields |
| No preceding observation exists within the search period; the grid falls after the last observation at or before `to`, within a recorded gap, or across an invalid input | Empty value at the unchanged grid timestamp; the corresponding missing reason |
| Grid falls between the observation that `getDataAtTime` supplies before `from` and the first raw observation of the range | Linear interpolation from that preceding observation; the preceding observation itself is not an export row |
| End time is not a grid point | No extra end row; all adjacent grid timestamps differ by the requested interval |
| Two PVs have different timestamps or one lacks a bracket | Independent calculations on one grid; no row shift and no value borrowed from another PV |
| Duplicate timestamp has conflicting data | Missing result at that timestamp and no interpolation through it |

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: accepted
Plan Acceptance: 2026-11-06; the complete plan with the M2 Address Contract, History Restoration Contract and Interpolated Export Contract, after nine review passes on 2026-11-06 (third-person on code, server operators and purpose; second-person from the implementer, T3 and T1 seats) with every accepted finding applied; D12-D14 unchanged; transitions on range changes deferred past M5. Revised and accepted again on 2026-11-06, before step 5: the M2 Address Contract follows the standards of its parts and is declared in `docs/analyzer-address.openapi.json`, after two third-person passes with both findings applied
Implementation Authorization: 2026-11-06; explicit owner instruction to proceed with the accepted six-step plan, each step committed with its own evidence rows
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

Each step is committed on its own with the T1-T6 cases it actually ran, recorded as evidence rows named by label and step (for example T1-2 for the T1 cases of step 2) under Verification Results; a label T1-T6 passes only when every case of its row has run, and the Analyzer stays usable after each step.

1. Review the complete interaction, address and export contracts, preserving the Accepted Defaults and `design-analyzer.md` D12-D14. Record complete-plan acceptance and implementation authorization separately before implementation. Check: every Scope control maps to the M2 Address Contract and a real-path test.
2. Integrate D3 zoom, brush and drag in `staticcontent/js/aa/chart/gestures.js`, `canvas.js` and `widget.js`; add the active-tool controls, history, pins and cursors through caller-owned state. Preserve the simple console behavior and `design-chart-canvas.md` ownership boundaries. Check: T1.
3. Extend `options.js`, `draw.js`, `hover.js` and `statistics.js` for display options (the current Trend tooltip is built inline in `draw.js` and does not call the shared `hover.js`; connect it to the shared hover so tooltip modes, pins and cursors have one implementation), optional line-end names, split axes and separate Summary/Visible range; retain the shared running-statistics sample window. In `retrieval/staticcontent/viewer/viewer.js`, separate source records, selection state and display state; connect interval and min/max retrieval without duplicate completion events. Check: T1, T2.
4. Complete Data and Export in `viewer.js` and narrowly scoped modules where needed. Preserve timestamp/status metadata, use D3 CSV formatting, implement the Interpolated Export Contract, connect PNG/SVG and supported server-streamed products, and expose effective optimized-export intervals. Check: T3.
5. Implement the M2 Address Contract in `chart/state.js` and the viewer address handling: typed parsing, identity-bound overrides, independent selected/display queries, axis policies, reference precision and export form state. Restore normalized state before retrieval; keep legacy links compatible and add real packaged reader/writer checks for every contract row. Check: T4.
6. Update the canvas contract and book to the actual shipped behavior, with concise information cards for calculation details. Use the prepared `scripts/build-book.sh` path, which shares its immutable `jeonghanlee/mdbook` image digest with `.github/workflows/pages.yml`, builds into isolated output and runs `scripts/check-book-links.pl`. Keep missing-preprocessor failures enabled. Rebuild the offline mockup from the production chart modules and demonstrate independent time/numeric instances. Check: T1, T5, T6.

#### M3 - Analyzer phase C: tools for one signal

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M6
Identity History: M6 to M3 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #39 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/39)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

The Browse PVs page shows a distribution, a scatter plot of two PVs and a statistics table for the PVs of one chart (`plots.js`), and the viewer none. The scatter plot pairs only samples at exactly equal times, so raw samples of two records rarely pair, and it offers only a PV of the same unit at a fixed 150 px height. The bundled D3 has no correlation, regression, fit, FFT or principal component analysis. The analysis of one numeric signal and the comparison of two ranges are built in this phase on the page of phases A and B (M1, M2), from what the D3 bundle provides and from routines the page writes; two and several signals are phase D (M4).

##### Scope

Phase C of `docs/design-analyzer.md` (D5): the tools of one numeric signal and the comparison of two ranges, in the Distribution, Compare and Spectrum tabs of the page of phases A and B (design, Tools by kind of signal; Analysis functions).

- Distribution tab: the summary (samples, minimum, maximum, mean, median, standard deviation, skewness, kurtosis, the errors of the mean and of the standard deviation, quantiles); the histogram with the `bin` threshold rules, the box plot, a kernel density estimate; the statistics box and, when a fit of phase D exists, the fit box; the pattern by hour and weekday (`rollup`) and a time-value heatmap for a long span; the values beyond k standard deviations.
- Trend tab additions: the rate of change (the value change per second between successive samples of the displayed dataset) and a slow trend (a linear fit against time); the running average and the standard deviation band exist since phase A and are not rebuilt; the limits of the PV (display, warning and alarm) as reference lines with the time outside them, read from `getMetadata` when it carries them (to confirm in step 1); a threshold line typed by the user with the time beyond it.
- Compare tab: two time ranges of one signal, or two signals, side by side with their ranges linked, the distributions drawn over one another and normalised, the difference of the means and of the spreads, and the tests (chi-square, Kolmogorov-Smirnov, Anderson-Darling).
- Spectrum tab: the FFT after resampling to an even interval (radix 2 with zero padding, or Bluestein), the window functions, the power spectral density, the peak frequency with its width, the spectrogram over time, the autocorrelation; the resampling interval typed, or by default the median spacing of the retrieved samples.
- The routines the page writes for these: moments, moving statistics, the tests, the FFT, the kernel density estimate; each checked on signals of known parameters made with the `random*` generators.
- The address names of the tabs' options; the fixture records the checks need (a signal with a known distribution and a known period); the book.
- Input suitability checks developed with each tool: metadata establishes supported data forms when a PV is selected; retrieved observations establish finite sample counts, gaps, time spacing, units and the selected method's prerequisites before calculation. Unknown measurement meaning or statistical assumptions require explicit user input; numeric type or a familiar PV name does not establish them. Known incompatibility has a specific reason and prevents calculation.

Out of scope: two and several signals, fits, derived signals (M4); waveforms and images, including the spectrum of a waveform with its sampling frequency typed or read from a companion PV (M5); state and string signals (after the numeric tools).

##### Completion Criteria

- Each tool of the Scope is available in its tab and is observed on archived fixture data.
- The numbers agree in two layers. Against an independent calculation (Python with SciPy 1.15.3, a validation dependency only) on fixed datasets: the moments and their errors, the quantiles, the chi-square, Kolmogorov-Smirnov and Anderson-Darling statistics, the periodogram power spectral density and the autocorrelation agree within 1e-9 relative, and the p-values within 1e-6 absolute (the Kolmogorov-Smirnov statistic equals that of `ks_2samp`, and its p-value is the classical asymptotic one, the Kolmogorov distribution at the square root of n times m over n plus m, times the statistic, compared with `scipy.special.kolmogorov` at that argument; the `method='asymp'` of `ks_2samp` uses a finite-sample distribution and is not the reference; the tab says that the p-value is asymptotic), except the p-value of Anderson-Darling, which is reported as SciPy reports it (an interval floored at 0.001 and capped at 0.25). Against signals of known parameters made with the seeded `random*` generators and the fixture: the mean and the standard deviation of a normal sample lie within 4 standard errors of the parameters, the spectrum peak of a sine lies within one frequency bin of its frequency, and the autocorrelation peak within one sample of its period. A time series is resampled to an even interval before an FFT.
- The limits of a PV are drawn as reference lines when `getMetadata` carries them, and the tool says that the PV has none when it does not.
- The Compare tab shows two ranges with their ranges linked and reports the tests with their statistics.
- Each new tool checks its input prerequisites on the selected data; unsuitable or incomplete input cannot leave a successful or stale result visible (T5).
- The default suite passes, the routine checks pass in the Maven workflow, and the book describes the tools.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- M2: the page with its gestures, options, Data and Export tabs, which the tools extend.
- D2: the analyzer as a self-contained analysis tool built with the bundled D3.
- The decisions of M59 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) on the bundled D3 (its functions are used wherever it has one), on the analyzer as a general signal analysis tool, and on no external library bind this work; the page writes only what the bundle lacks.
- D5 (Decision Date 2026-10-19): phase C of the analyzer, planned from `docs/design-analyzer.md`; the tools of two and several signals, the fits and the derived signals are phase D (M4).
- Decision Date 2026-10-14: the tools are added by the kind of signal; the page decides the kind from the type information and the user chooses the purpose within it.
- Decision Date 2026-11-07: M3 stays one row, worked in steps by tab, each step with its own checks and commit as M2 was; the spectrum of a waveform, with its sampling frequency typed or read from a companion PV, belongs to M5, which already carries it, so the Spectrum tab of this row takes a scalar time series. The tolerances of the Completion Criteria and the layout of the routine checks are the values proposed by the plan review for the owner's acceptance.
- Read in step 1 (2026-11-07, row T6-1), the hypotheses of the plan review of 2026-11-07: the limits of a PV come from `getMetadata` (`HIHI`, `HIGH`, `LOW`, `LOLO`, `LOPR`, `HOPR`, with `NaN` for a limit that is not set and 0 and 0 for a display range that is not set), while `getData.json` carries only `EGU`, `PREC` and `name`; the runner holds the generated multi-day data and the retrieval returns it unchanged. The lost-event count of the summary has no source for a range: the counters of `getPVDetails` count from the start of the engine. Decision Date 2026-11-07: the summary does not show lost events, because no source gives them for a selected range, and a count of another period beside the statistics of the range would mislead; the counters stay in the Details of the PV.
- Decision Date 2026-11-04: input checks are implemented and verified with these tabs and Trend additions, following the Input suitability checks contract of `docs/design-analyzer-ui.md`; metadata classifies data form, not physical process or distribution. This work does not block M1.
- Decision Date 2026-11-09: the options of the Distribution, Compare and Spectrum tabs and of the Trend tools are part of the address, so that a copied address opens the same result. Step 6 declares them in `docs/analyzer-address.openapi.json` as one deepObject parameter per tab named as the view (`distribution[bins]`, `trend[threshold]`, `compare[method]`, `spectrum[window]`), and the page reads and writes them as it does the Export choices: an invalid value falls back to its default, a default is not written, a pair applies whole or not at all. The declaration alone would break the promise of the address document that the page writes every parameter back, so the step carries the page change and its browser checks (T1 and T5 of step 6) beside T3 and T4.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: accepted
Plan Acceptance: 2026-11-07; the owner accepted the reviewed plan with the tolerances and the layout of the routine checks that the plan review proposed (three review passes after the plan review, Open 0)
Implementation Authorization: 2026-11-07; explicit instruction to implement the accepted plan
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

Each step is committed on its own with the T cases it ran, recorded as evidence rows named by label and step (for example T2-2 for the T2 cases of step 2) under Verification Results; a label T1-T6 passes only when every case of its row has run, and the Analyzer stays usable after each step.

1. The ground of the checks. (a) The fixture: records with signals of known parameters (a pure sine of known period and amplitude beside the existing noisy ones), and a generated multi-day signal with a known normal distribution and a daily and weekly pattern, written through the shipped PlainPB writer into the store of the run so that the appliance serves it through `getData.json`. (b) The routine check path: Node's built-in test runner (`node --test`, no new dependency) over `src/test/js/analyzer/` with the bundled D3 as global, run by the new `scripts/ui-routine-checks.bash` and added to the Maven workflow beside the static checks; the JDK 21 job runs on the ubuntu runner, which has Node, and the Debian and Rocky container jobs run it only when their installed Node is 20.19, 22.7 or later (the versions that read ES module syntax in a `.js` file), which the first workflow run shows. (c) The two reads of the Dependencies: `getMetadata` of the fixture PVs for the limit fields and the source of the lost-event count; record them in this detail. Checks: T6, T2, T4.
2. The routines the page writes (new modules under `staticcontent/js/aa/`): moments and their errors, quantiles, the kernel density estimate, the tests with their p-values, the resampling and the FFT with its windows, the periodogram, the autocorrelation; each with its checks against the SciPy values stored beside the fixed datasets and on seeded generated signals. Check: T2.
3. The Distribution tab and the Trend additions (rate of change, slow trend, limits, threshold) through the component and the D3 functions of the decision of M59 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71), with their numeric-data, sample-count and method checks. Checks: T1, T5.
4. The Compare tab as a split view with linked ranges, with unit, sample-coverage and statistical-method checks. Checks: T1, T5.
5. The Spectrum tab, with explicit timing, gap and resampling prerequisites and a reason when the selected input is unsuitable. Checks: T1, T2, T5.
6. The address names of the options of the four tools (declared in `docs/analyzer-address.openapi.json` and read and written by the page, Decision 2026-11-09), the help page, `design-analyzer-ui.md`, the list of the summary in `design-analyzer.md` (without lost events) and the book. Checks: T1, T3, T4, T5.

- Implementation rechecked against the accepted plan and its completion criteria on 2026-11-09: the six steps landed in order (the fixture and the check path; the routines; the Distribution tab and the Trend tools; the Compare tab; the Spectrum tab; the options of the tools in the address with the help page, the design documents and the book). Each tool of the Scope is in its tab and observed on the archived fixture data (T1-3, T1-4, T1-5, T1-6). The numbers agree in the two layers: against SciPy and NumPy on the fixed datasets within the tolerances (T2-2 to T2-5, with the asymptotic Kolmogorov-Smirnov p-value and the floored and capped Anderson-Darling significance as stated), and on the signals of known parameters (the normal sample within 4 standard errors in T2-2, the peak of the sine within one bin of 1/600 Hz and the autocorrelation period of exactly 60 samples in T1-5 and T2-5). The limits of a PV are drawn when `getMetadata` carries them and the tool says that a PV has none (T1-3, SR:BPM:01:X). The Compare tab links its two ranges and reports the tests with their statistics or the reason a test does not apply (T1-4, T5-4). Every tool checks its prerequisites and leaves no stale result (T5-3 to T5-6). The default suite, the static checks and the routine checks pass locally on the final tree (T4-6) and in the Maven workflow on the commits of the steps, and the book describes the tools (T3-6). The lost-event count of the summary is not shown (Decision 2026-11-07).
