#### M4 - Analyzer phase D: tools for two and several signals

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M12
Identity History: M12 to M4 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #39 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/39)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

Phase D of `docs/design-analyzer.md` (D5): the tools of two and of several numeric signals, the fits and the derived signals, in the Correlation and Dependency tabs of the page of phases A to C (design, Tools by kind of signal; Analysis functions).

##### Scope

- Correlation tab: a scatter plot of two signals with each axis in its own unit, the samples paired by the value each holds at the other's sample times (`bisector`), with the number of pairs and of samples of the slower signal; the correlation (Pearson, Spearman through `rank`, a cross-correlation over a lag); a profile (the mean of Y with its error in each bin of X); the fit panel after the reference tools: a model chosen (line, polynomial, exponential, power, logarithm, sine, Gaussian, Landau), initial values, fixed and bounded parameters, a range by sliders, the parameters with their errors, the chi-square per degree of freedom, R², a confidence band, the residuals, earlier fits kept in a list; X and Y projections beside the axes; point size and transparency, points coloured by time, a density contour (`contourDensity`) for dense data, a rectangle brush; the two trends under one another sharing the time axis; derived signals (difference, sum, ratio). X and Y are chosen independently with Swap; the pairing is a visible processing setting, and the pair count, each input's sample count, the exclusions and the usable time overlap are shown with the result; a pairing never conceals a period of unknown connection state; a failed or underdetermined fit shows no curve and no numeric success.
- Dependency tab, for three to twenty signals averaged over one common interval: the mutual information of each pair and a ranking by how much each explains the chosen one; a correlation matrix as a heatmap ordered by hierarchical clustering with its dendrogram; a scatter plot matrix; the partial correlation; a multiple linear regression with each coefficient, its standardised size, R² and the residuals; a principal component analysis with the variance of each component. The matrix is shown first; a selected cell opens the Correlation tab with the same range and the two signals of the cell; the common interval, the alignment policy, the sample coverage and the exclusion count are shown with the result; the ranking is worded as statistical association, never as causation; an analysis too large or too poorly conditioned offers a smaller selection or another interval instead of a blank result.
- General functions: a derived signal from a typed expression parsed by the page, a change of unit, the export of a fit, a statistics table and a derived signal through the Export tab.
- The routines the page writes: least squares and an iterative least squares (Levenberg-Marquardt) with fixed and bounded parameters, the covariance of the parameters, correlation and partial correlation, mutual information (a binned estimate with a stated number of equal-width bins), a small matrix module (multiply, invert, solve, Cholesky, Jacobi eigen decomposition), hierarchical clustering (average linkage), the expression parser; each checked against SciPy and NumPy on fixed datasets and on signals of known parameters.
- The fixture records the checks need: generated signals of known relationship (a target that is a linear combination of several generated signals plus noise with known coefficients, a pair of known correlation, a pair sampled at different rates for the pairing), written through the shipped PlainPB writer as the signals of M3 are.
- The address names of the options of the two tabs (`correlation[...]` and `dependency[...]`, read and written by the page as the options of M3's tools are, Decision 2026-11-09 of M3), the help page, the design documents and the book.
- Input suitability checks developed with Correlation, Dependency and the fit tools, including histogram fitting: supported numeric inputs, usable overlap, pair or common-sample counts, constant inputs, model domains, parameter bounds and identifiability. Count models require explicit count interpretation and suitable observations; integers alone do not establish a Poisson process. Model suitability and uncertainty assumptions remain visible user choices where the data cannot establish them.

Out of scope: one signal and the comparison (M3); waveforms and images (M5); a vendored library for LOESS or a non-linear fit (Decision 2026-11-09: none is vendored); a typed formula as a fit model (Decision 2026-11-09: a later row after M5; the expression parser serves the derived signals only); a graph of dependence drawn with `forceSimulation` (Decision 2026-11-09: not a view of the designs; the clustered matrix and its dendrogram carry that information).

##### Completion Criteria

- Each tool of the Scope is available in its tab and is observed on archived fixture data; a pair of different rates shows the number of pairs and of samples of the slower signal.
- The numbers agree in two layers. Against an independent calculation (Python with SciPy 1.15.3 and NumPy, validation dependencies only) on fixed datasets: the linear least squares (line, polynomial, exponential, power and logarithm in their transformed variable), Pearson and Spearman correlation, the cross-correlation, the partial correlation, the coefficients and R² of the multiple regression and the eigenvalues and components of the principal component analysis agree within 1e-9 relative; the parameters of the iterative fits (sine, Gaussian, Landau) agree with SciPy `curve_fit` started from the same initial values within 1e-6 relative or within a tenth of the parameter's standard error, whichever is larger, and their errors within 1e-6 relative; the mutual information agrees with the same binned estimator in NumPy within 1e-9; the clustering order equals SciPy `linkage` with average linkage. Against the generated signals of known relationship: each fitted or regressed parameter lies within 4 standard errors of the value it was made with, the correlation of the pair of known correlation within 4 standard errors, and in the principal component analysis of the linear combination the components beyond the number of generating signals carry a variance at the level of the added noise. The fit panel fixes and bounds a parameter, and a fixed parameter keeps its value.
- The Dependency tab takes up to twenty signals; the matrix is symmetric with ones on its diagonal and its order follows the clustering.
- Each implemented analysis and fit checks its own input and model prerequisites, explains failure or missing required information, and clears stale results when inputs change (T6).
- The default suite passes, the routine checks pass in the Maven workflow, and the book describes the tools.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- M3: the tools of one signal and the routines it writes (moments, tests, FFT), which the fits and the dependency tools use.
- D2: the analyzer as a self-contained analysis tool built with the bundled D3; no regression package is added (`d3-regression` is outside the official D3 repositories).
- D5 (Decision Date 2026-10-19): phase D of the analyzer, planned from `docs/design-analyzer.md`.
- Decision Date 2026-10-12: the correlation and the linear regression are computed by the page; the bundled D3 has neither.
- Decision Date 2026-10-14: the fit models are those listed; the Correlation tab opens with the first two plotted PVs as X and Y; the dependency analysis takes up to 20 PVs.
- Decision Date 2026-11-04: implement and verify input and model checks with the corresponding tab or fit tool, following the Input suitability checks contract of `docs/design-analyzer-ui.md`; this is not an additional M1 prerequisite.
- Decision Date 2026-11-09: no library is vendored for LOESS or a non-linear fit; the page writes the Levenberg-Marquardt fit as the design says, since D2 and R3 allow only an official D3 module to be vendored. A typed formula as a fit model is a later row after M5: the expression parser of this row serves the derived signals, and a fit model from it would need a derivative, initial values and an identifiability check for each expression. The graph of dependence drawn with `forceSimulation` is not a view of the two design documents and is left out; the clustered correlation matrix with its dendrogram carries the same information.
- Decision Date 2026-11-09: the three items that `docs/design-analyzer-ui.md` leaves to this plan. The gap rule of the pairing: a value of the other signal is paired only when it is not older than five times the median spacing of that signal and no recorded connection loss lies between it and the sample time; a sample without such a value is left out and counted. The weighting: a fit of the scatter plot is unweighted; a fit of a histogram uses Poisson weights (the residual of each bin divided by the square root of its count, so that its squared residual carries the weight one over the count; empty bins left out) once the user has declared the count interpretation; no other weights are taken. The uncertainty assumptions: the parameter errors come from the covariance of the fit and, for an unweighted fit, are scaled by the reduced chi-square (as SciPy `curve_fit` does without absolute errors); the fit result states this beside the errors.
- Decision Date 2026-11-09 (step 4): the derived signals live in the Correlation tab, computed from the pairs by a typed expression over x and y (or the PV names) and drawn as a third trend under the two, exported through the Export tab; a page-wide derived signal in the Trend would break the promise of the address that `pv` names archived PVs. The address carries the fit model and the fit range of the Correlation tab and the fit model of the Distribution tab; start values, fixed parameters and bounds restore from the form only, and a fit runs from its button, not from the address. The fit range is a pair of typed bounds, not the sliders the Scope names after ROOT's fit editor: typed bounds restore exactly from the address, and the UI design asks for a fit range only. The fit of a histogram evaluates the model at the bin centres against the bin heights with Poisson weights, empty bins left out: the residual of each bin is divided by the square root of its count, so the squared residual carries the weight one over the count, which is how the page, its checkbox and the reference generator state it; withdrawing the declaration clears the fit; the weights do not apply to a model fitted in a transformed variable (exponential, power), and the method row says so; R squared of a weighted fit is computed from the unweighted residuals, and its row says so. The residuals of the Correlation fit are drawn under the fit result over the width of the tab. The box says all this; the expected count integrated over the bin edges is a later candidate.
- Decision Date 2026-11-10 (step 5): the Dependency tab averages the signals over one common interval, that of the page (the automatic interval of the range when the page shows raw samples) or one chosen in the tab from 10 s to 6 h, retrieves the means of each signal through the endpoint the page uses, four at a time, and aligns them on the intervals that every signal has; the others are dropped and counted, with the share of the intervals each signal has. A signal without numeric means, a signal that does not vary over the common intervals, fewer than 10 common intervals and more than 100,000 intervals in the range are refused with their reason and a way out, and so are fewer than three or more than twenty signals. The ranking and the mutual information matrix (the Scope names the mutual information of each pair beside the ranking against the target) use the binned plug-in estimate over 16 equal-width bins, as the reference file does; the scatter plot matrix shows the target and the five signals most associated with it, at most 300 points each; the partial correlation and the regression are refused, naming the pair that moves together, when the matrix cannot be inverted, and a condition number above 1,000 is written beside every result as a warning. The address carries `dependency[target]`, `[view]` and `[interval]` (brought forward from step 6 as for the Correlation tab), and a cell of a matrix opens the Correlation tab with the same range and the two signals of the cell.
- Decision Date 2026-11-10 (change of unit): the Scope names a change of unit, and the owner chose on that day to build it before the closure of M4 instead of leaving it to the derived signal. The unit of X and of Y in the Correlation tab can each be shown in another prefix of the same base unit as the engineering unit of the signal (mm in um, V in mV, kHz in MHz; the bases metre, volt, ampere, watt, hertz, second, tesla, pascal, joule, radian, ohm and kelvin with the prefixes in use for each): the values are multiplied by a power of ten, and the pairs, the correlation, the fit with its parameters and errors, the trends and the derived signal use the converted values. A unit that is not a prefix and one of these bases (a compound such as mm/s, degC, Gs, which is gauss and not a gigasecond, an empty text) offers no change, so that nothing is converted by a guess; no conversion with an offset or between different units (degC to K, inch to mm) is made. A change of the unit clears a fit made in the other unit; Swap exchanges the units with the signals; the address carries `correlation[xUnit]` and `correlation[yUnit]` and drops a unit that the signal does not offer; the fit and the derived signal CSV name the unit of X and of Y. The Dependency tab, whose standardised numbers do not depend on the unit, keeps the units as stored.
- Decision Date 2026-11-09: M4 stays one row, worked in steps as M3 was, each step with its own checks and commit; the tolerances of the Completion Criteria and the layout of the checks (the reference file of SciPy and NumPy values beside the fixed datasets, the routine checks under Node's test runner, the browser drivers on the generated fixture) are those of M3.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: accepted
Plan Acceptance: 2026-11-09; the owner accepted the reviewed plan with the decisions of 2026-11-09 (no vendored fit library, the typed fit model as a later row, no dependence graph, the gap rule, the weighting and the uncertainty assumptions, one row worked in steps) and the tolerances of the Completion Criteria (four review passes after the plan review, Open 0)
Implementation Authorization: 2026-11-09; explicit instruction to implement the accepted plan
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

Each step is committed on its own with the T cases it ran, recorded as evidence rows named by label and step (for example T2-2 for the T2 cases of step 2) under Verification Results; a label T1-T7 passes only when every case of its row has run, and the Analyzer stays usable after each step.

1. The ground of the checks: the generated signals of known relationship in `AnalyzerFixtureData` (a target as a linear combination of several signals plus noise, a pair of known correlation, a pair at different rates) written into the store of the run and retrieved through `getData.json`; the sections of the reference file for the fits, the correlations, the regression, the principal component analysis, the mutual information and the clustering, written by `generate-references.py` with SciPy and NumPy. Checks: T7, T2 (the reference file).
2. The routines under `staticcontent/js/aa/`: the matrix module, the linear and the iterative least squares with fixed and bounded parameters and the covariance, the correlations, the mutual information, the clustering and the expression parser, each with its checks against the reference file and on the generated signals. Check: T2.
3. The Correlation tab without the fit panel: X and Y with Swap, the pairing with its gap rule, counts and overlap, the scatter plot with projections, point size, transparency, colour by time, the density contour and the brush, the correlations, the profile and the two trends under one another; the pairing, overlap and constant-input checks. Checks: T1, T6.
4. The fit panel of the Correlation tab (models, initial values, fixed and bounded parameters, range, parameters with errors, chi-square per degree of freedom, R², confidence band, residuals, the list of earlier fits), the fit box of the Distribution tab (a histogram fit with Poisson weights once the count interpretation is declared by the user) and the derived signals from the expression parser; the model-domain, parameter, identifiability and count-interpretation checks. Checks: T1, T6.
5. The Dependency tab for three to twenty signals over one common interval: the matrix ordered by the clustering with its dendrogram, the ranking by mutual information, the scatter plot matrix, the partial correlation, the multiple regression and the principal component analysis, a cell opening the Correlation tab; the coverage, constant-input, rank and conditioning checks. Checks: T3, T6.
6. The address names of the options of the two tabs, read and written by the page and declared in `docs/analyzer-address.openapi.json`, the help page, `design-analyzer-ui.md`, `design-analyzer.md` and the book. Checks: T1, T3, T4, T5, T6.

#### M5 - Analyzer phase E: waveform and image analysis

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M7
Identity History: M7 to M5 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #39 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/39)
Status: Not started

##### Summary

The appliance stores an array PV as a waveform (`DBR_WAVEFORM_*`, or `DBR_V4_GENERIC_BYTES` for a PVA structure); it holds no image size, and the analyzer reads numeric scalar samples only today: an array sample is kept as text and gives no point (`viewer.js`, `fetchSeries`). The camera IOC of the site serves row and column profiles of 1920 and 1200 elements, time-series arrays of 2048 points, a histogram of 256 bins and the statistics of up to four regions of interest, and a frame of 1920 by 1200 sixteen-bit pixels at up to 50 Hz. The analysis of arrays and images is built in this phase on the page of phases A and B (M1, M2) and on the tools of phases C and D (M3, M4).

##### Scope

The analysis of waveforms and images in the analyzer.

Input suitability checks are developed with these tools: validate the actual selected array, its axis meaning, companion values, image dimensions and region bounds. A numeric array is not automatically an image or a time waveform; generic array display by element index remains available, while a tool requiring physical axis meaning, sample interval or dimensions requests missing information from the user. The archive event rate is not the sampling rate within an array.

- One waveform (an array PV): the array at a time, X as the element number or from a companion axis PV; several times overlaid, and the time against the element number as a heatmap; summary values of each array (maximum and its position, minimum, mean, RMS, sum, centre, FWHM) and their time series; the difference from a reference time; the spectrum by FFT with the sampling frequency given by the user or a companion PV, a window function and the peak frequency; a peak search with the position, height and width of each peak, and a background estimated and subtracted.
- An image (a waveform whose element count is width times height, the size given by the user or companion PVs): one frame as a heatmap on a canvas with a colour scale and iso-lines; a rectangle of interest chosen with the mouse; the X and Y projections; the centroid, the peak position, the total intensity and the size by a Gaussian fit in each direction; those values as time series; a background subtraction and the difference of two frames.
- Settled on 2026-10-14 on the recommendation the owner accepted:
  - A waveform: the X axis is the element number, or the array of a companion axis PV when the user names one; the length and the rate are read from `getPVTypeInfo` (the element count and the event rate). The camera IOC of the site, read for this plan, serves row and column profiles of 1920 and 1200 elements, time-series arrays of 2048 points, a histogram of 256 bins and the statistics of up to four regions of interest, so the analysis is built for arrays of that size first.
  - An image: the width and the height come from the companion PVs of the same array plugin (`ArraySize0_RBV` and `ArraySize1_RBV`, with `NDimensions_RBV`, `DataType_RBV` and `ColorMode_RBV`), and the user types them when those are not archived. The camera IOC of the site serves a frame of 1920 by 1200 sixteen-bit pixels (2304000 elements) at up to 50 Hz, so the archived data is expected to be the regional statistics and profiles, and a whole frame is read one at a time.

Out of scope: the page, drawing options and address of the analyzer (M1); the tools for numeric signals (M3); what the site archives of a camera, which the settings of the archiver decide; the export of an array, a frame or an image result as a product of the Export tab, which the UI design names for an image and which stays outside this row by the owner's decision of 2026-11-10 (a later row if it is asked for).

##### Completion Criteria

- A waveform is drawn at a time, overlaid and as a heatmap over time; its summary values, their time series, its difference from a reference time, its spectrum and its peaks with a background subtracted are shown.
- An image of a known size is drawn as a heatmap with a colour scale and iso-lines, its region of interest chosen with the mouse and typed, its projections and its centroid, peak, total and Gaussian size computed, and a frame is read one at a time in the orientation chosen; the values over selected frames are a separate request with progress and cancel.
- The values agree in two layers, with the tolerances of M3 and M4. Against an independent calculation (Python with SciPy 1.15.3 and NumPy, validation dependencies only) on fixed arrays and images: the sum, mean, RMS, centre, centroid, projections, difference and heatmap cells agree within 1e-9 relative, the positions and widths of the peaks (SciPy `find_peaks` and `peak_widths`), the half-maximum width and the background (SciPy `minimum_filter1d` and `maximum_filter1d`) within 1e-6, and the Gaussian size of a projection with SciPy `curve_fit` as in M4 (1e-6 relative or a tenth of the error). Against arrays and images of known content: a sine peaks at its frequency within one bin, and a Gaussian blob returns its centre and its width within four standard errors.
- Each tool validates the selected frame and its required axis or dimensions, rejects incompatible lengths and regions with a reason, never takes the event rate of the archive as the sample rate within an array, infers no image from the length of an array alone, and clears results when frame metadata or selection changes (T6).
- The options of the two tabs are in the address and are declared in `docs/analyzer-address.openapi.json`, an invalid field keeps its default, and a default is not written (T8).
- The default suite passes, and the book, the help page and the design documents describe the tools (T4, T5).

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- M4 and M3: the numeric tools (fits, FFT, statistics) that arrays and images use.
- M1 and M2: the analyzer page, its gestures, options and address.
- D5 (Decision Date 2026-10-19): phase E of the analyzer, planned from `docs/design-analyzer.md` (Waveforms and images).
- D2: the analyzer as a self-contained analysis tool built with the bundled D3.
- The decisions of M59 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) on the bundled D3, on the analyzer as a general signal analysis tool and on no external library bind this work; a frame is drawn on a canvas with the D3 colour scales and `contours`, the axes and text staying SVG.
- The retrieval response for an array PV and the size limit of one response are read on the fixture before the page is built.
- Decision Date 2026-11-04: implement and verify array, axis and image-dimension checks when developing Waveform and Image, following the Input suitability checks contract of `docs/design-analyzer-ui.md`; this work is not required to complete M1.
- Decision Date 2026-11-10 (plan review, accepted on the recommendation of the owner): (1) the frames of a range for the overlay, the heatmap over time and the series of summary values are the raw arrays thinned by the server (the operator `nth`, whose use on array types the fixture confirms in step 1), at most 200 frames per range: the page takes N from the event rate of the type information (`getPVTypeInfo`), and when more than 200 frames arrive, because the rate of the archive varies, it thins them again on the page and names the step it took (every Nth of the archive and every Mth of those) and offers a shorter range; a range whose size estimate the server refuses is reported with that reason; a single frame is read at its time (`getDataAtTime` where it returns arrays, else a narrow `getData.json` window, as step 1 finds). (2) The algorithms: the centre is the intensity-weighted centroid, defined when the sum of the intensities is positive and otherwise stated as not defined, with no value clamped; the half-maximum width is measured at half of the maximum above the minimum of the array, by linear interpolation of the two crossings nearest the maximum, one on each side, and not defined (and said to be) when the array stays above the half level on one side; the peak search takes the local maxima, as `find_peaks` does (the middle of a plateau counts, and neither end of the array is a peak), whose prominence reaches a fraction (5 percent by default) of the range of the array after the background is subtracted, and reports the position, the height above the background and the width at half the prominence; the background is the morphological opening (a minimum filter then a maximum filter) over a window the user sets, 10 percent of the length by default, subtracted from the array; the Gaussian size of an image is the Gaussian model of M4 fitted to the X and to the Y projection of the region, the width being sigma and the full width at half maximum 2 sqrt(2 ln 2) sigma (about 2.355 sigma); an image is row-major with the origin at the top left, the column index running along X; the Image tab offers Flip X and Flip Y as display options of the orientation, and the region is always given in the stored indices. (3) The settings of an array or an image (the companion axis, the sampling frequency, the width and the height) can be typed, and a selected PV can be given each role; a PV of a role is read at the frame time (its last value at or before it), and the sampling frequency is never the event rate of the archive. (4) What the site archives is not assumed: a frame is read one at a time, the array tools take whatever array is archived (a profile, a time series of 2048 points, a histogram), and the values over frames are limited to 100 selected frames with progress and cancel. (5) The work is six steps, each committed with its T rows as M3 and M4 were.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: draft
Plan Acceptance: none
Implementation Authorization: none
Superseded Plan Artifacts: the draft of 2026-10-14 (five steps), replaced in this register on 2026-11-10 after the plan review

1. The ground of the checks and the reading of arrays: `AnalyzerFixtureData` writes waveform events of known content (a 2048-element array with a sine and peaks on a background, a time series of arrays, and an image of 640 by 480 pixels with a Gaussian blob of known centre and width, which T3 and T6 use (a size that keeps the checks fast), with the companion records for the width, the height, the sampling frequency and the axis in `AnalyzerToolPVs.db`), a frame of 1920 by 1200 sixteen-bit pixels is generated once for the measurement alone, the measurement of the refusal uses a range long enough that the server estimates its size as too large, and the sections of the reference file for the arrays and the images are written by `generate-references.py` with NumPy and SciPy. The harness of the checks (`scripts/ui-appliance.sh`, which exports the Channel Access variables of the private network namespace) sets `EPICS_CA_MAX_ARRAY_BYTES` to 16000000 for the soft IOC and for the engine (a 2048-element array of doubles is 16384 bytes, the default limit, and a frame of 2,304,000 pixels is 4.6 MB as 16-bit values and 9.2 MB as 32-bit values), and the element count of the type information of each array PV is checked against the `NELM` of its record. The archiver has `DBR_WAVEFORM_SHORT` (16 bits, signed) and `DBR_WAVEFORM_INT` (32 bits) and no unsigned 16-bit type, so the type that the type information gives a record of unsigned 16-bit pixels after archiving is read first, the generator writes events of that type, and the test image holds pixel values above 32767 to show that none is changed. The retrieval response of an array and of an image frame is read on the fixture: the size of one response, the time to parse a frame, the use of `nth` on array types and of `getDataAtTime`, and the reply of the server to a range whose estimated size is too large. Checks: T1, T7 (the reference file).
2. The routines under `staticcontent/js/aa/`: `array.js` (summary values, centre, half-maximum width, difference from a reference, the grid of the heatmap, the background by opening and the peak search) and `image.js` (the region, the projections, the centroid, the peak, the total, the background subtraction, the difference of two frames and the Gaussian size through `aa/fit.js`), each with its checks against the reference file and on the arrays and images of known content. Check: T7.
3. The reading of arrays and the first part of the Waveform tab: a dataset of arrays (the elements as typed arrays, the frames thinned by `nth` to at most 200, a single frame at its time), the array at a time with previous and next frame and a slider over the available times, the overlay, the time against the element heatmap, the summary values and their series, the companion axis and the typed settings; the array, axis and frame checks; the check of the Distribution driver of M3 that names the Waveform tab as the unavailable one (the copy in `work/analyzer-m4-verification/step5/regression/step3-driver-waveform.cjs`) names the Image tab instead, which stays unavailable until step 5. Checks: T2, T6.
4. The rest of the Waveform tab: the difference from a reference time, the spectrum by FFT with the sampling frequency typed or taken from a PV of that role, the window and the peak frequency, the peak search with the background; the checks of the sample interval and the window. Checks: T2, T6.
5. The Image tab on a canvas: one frame as a heatmap with the colour scale and iso-lines, the region of interest chosen with the mouse and typed, the projections, the centroid, peak, total and Gaussian size, the background subtraction and the difference of two frames, the values over at most 100 selected frames with progress and cancel; the Flip X and Flip Y of the orientation; the checks of the dimensions against the length, the region bounds and the reference frame; that check of the driver, which names the Image tab, names an invalid view instead, since no tab is unavailable any more. Checks: T3, T6.
6. The address names of the two tabs (`waveform[...]` and `image[...]`, read and written by `viewer/tool-address.js` and declared in `docs/analyzer-address.openapi.json`), the help page (`help.html`), `design-analyzer-ui.md`, `design-analyzer.md`, the book and the static checks, with the consistency check of the three sources of the names; the sentences of the book, the help page and the designs that say that the two tabs are not available are removed, and an address that names an invalid view keeps the presentation of an unavailable view. Checks: T8, T4, T5.

#### M6 - Appliance status as PVA PVs

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M4
Identity History: M4 to M6 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #66 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/66)
Status: Not started

##### Summary

The System pages show the appliance's own measures (event rate, dropped samples, write load, stored data and free space, process heap, host load). They are not archived PVs: the appliance keeps a few hours of the process measures in memory and serves them through mgmt calls, and the Operations tab draws them itself, so the viewer, which reads archived PVs from retrieval, cannot show them. The appliance already runs PVA servers: retrieval and mgmt create PVs on the `PVAServer` of `core-pva` (`retrieval/pva/PvaDataRetrievalServlet.java`, `mgmt/pva/`).

##### Scope

Publish the status measures as PVA PVs on the appliance's own PVA server, updated periodically, and archive them through `pva://` on the same appliance, so the analyzer plots them over any span.

- A stacked area view (`d3-shape` `stack`) of the event rate and of the stored data by store over a chosen span, drawn from the archived status PVs; it moved here from M56 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71), whose treemap shows the current values only.

Out of scope: a separate soft IOC fed from the mgmt calls; Channel Access names for the measures; monitoring whether the appliance runs, which stays outside it because a self-archived measure records nothing while the appliance is down.

##### Completion Criteria

- Each status measure the System pages show is a PVA PV with a defined name, type and update period, readable with a PVA client.
- The appliance archives them and the analyzer plots them from retrieval.
- The event rate and the stored data by store show as stacked areas over a chosen span, drawn from the archived PVs.
- An integration test reads the PVs and their archived data on a running appliance; the default suite passes.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- Origin: recorded on 2026-10-12 at the owner's direction, who chose publication by the appliance's own PVA server over a separate soft IOC.
- Decision Date 2026-10-13: the stacked areas of the event rate and the stored data over time are part of this item, because the history they need is what this item archives.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: draft
Plan Acceptance: none
Implementation Authorization: none
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

1. Settle with the owner the measures, their PV names and the update period.
2. Publish, archive and test them, and document the names. Closes with T1 and T2.

#### M8 - Bound the concurrent calls of the PVAccess retrieval service

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M14
Identity History: M14 to M8 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #62 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/62)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

The PVAccess server of `core-pva` 5.0.5 runs an RPC call on the receive thread of the TCP connection that carried it (executed on 2026-10-21 in the audit of M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71): a call of 2 s took 2,008 ms alone, two calls on one connection took 4,003 ms, two calls on two connections took 2,002 ms). It sets no limit on the connections and none on the calls at a time, and a long call holds the other calls of its connection. The Tomcat valves of M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) do not reach it, because the PVAccess server is not behind the Tomcat pipeline. M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) bounds a request by the memory it declares and the memory pressure, but the number of PVAccess retrievals in flight, each holding its answer whole (M86 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71)), is not bounded.

##### Scope

- The retrieval PVAccess service (`PvaDataRetrievalService`) takes a permit for each call and refuses the call when none is free, with a message in the log of the retrieval application. What the caller sees for a refused call is observed in T2: decision (4) of M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) and the M11 observation say its own timeout, while the library sends an error reply for an exception from `call`.
- The number of permits is read from a property of its own, separate from the valve concurrency. Its default is the valve default, 100, and is recorded as not derived from a load measurement (D8); the measurement belongs to M9. A permit count above the number of connections that call at the same time never refuses, because calls on one connection run one after the other.

Out of scope: the duration of a call, which no layer bounds in PVAccess; the management PVAccess service (`PvaMgmtService`); the HTTP valves (M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71)).

##### Completion Criteria

- With more long calls than permits, on separate connections, the permits are used and the next call is refused with a message in the log of the retrieval application.
- When a call ends, the next call is accepted.
- The number of permits is read from its own property: the default is 100 and a set value replaces it.
- The default suite and the PVAccess integration classes pass.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- D7 (Decision Date 2026-10-21): entered from the audit of M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71); it does not block M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) or the merge.
- Decided at the plan review (Decision Date 2026-11-02): the permit default is 100, as the valve default, without a load measurement; the property is its own and not the valve property; the duration of a call and the management service stay out of scope.
- Observed in T2 (2026-11-02): a refused call stays pending at the caller for the 10 s the test waits, as M11 observed, although the library sends an error reply for an exception from `call`; a call that takes long is made through the real path by holding the PB file reads of two day-long raw calls (`PbReadHold`) in the 512 MiB fixture.
- Below the floor, recorded for a later stage (third-person review of 2026-11-02, owner confirmed): the refusal writes one warning line per refused call, so a flood of refused calls writes as many lines; an invalid value of the property stops only the PVAccess service at servlet init, while an invalid memory setting stops the config service.
- Below the floor, recorded for a later stage (second-person review of 2026-11-02, owner confirmed): the book gives no rule for choosing a permit count other than the default; the load measurement that would support one belongs to M9.
- Below the floor, recorded for a later stage (third-person review of 2026-11-02, owner confirmed): two `assertTrue(!x)` calls in `PvaCallLimiterNetworkTest` read better as `assertFalse`.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: accepted
Plan Acceptance: 2026-11-02, after the plan review with three owner decisions and one re-review whose four findings are incorporated
Implementation Authorization: 2026-11-02, owner authorized implementing and verifying the accepted plan (steps 1 to 3); no commit or push is authorized by it
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

1. A limiter class with a fixed number of permits that never waits. Check: T1.
2. The retrieval service takes a permit around each call, before the memory request begins, and releases it in a `finally`. A refusal writes a message to the log of the retrieval application. Check: T1 and T2.
3. The number of permits comes from its own property with the default 100; the book page of retrieval states that the default is not derived from a load measurement and that a count above the simultaneous connections never refuses. The test T2 sets the property to a small number. The property takes one of the two existing prefixes, `archappl.valve.` or `org.epics.archiverappliance.retrieval.memory.`, and enters the table of properties of the book. Check: T1, T2 and the book link check.

#### M9 - Measure the thread and valve limits and the remaining qualification cells of the retrieval protection

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M16
Identity History: M16 to M9 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #58 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/58)
Status: Not started

##### Summary

The retrieval memory protection of M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) passed its functional checks on HTTP and PVAccess at 512 MiB, 2 GiB and 4 GiB under G1 with no OutOfMemoryError or JVM exit. Two groups of measurements remain. First, the thread and valve limits: the retrieval `SemaphoreValve` concurrency 100 and the connector `maxThreads` (Tomcat's default 200 in the deployment template, 40 in the development launcher) are not derived from a measurement; every busy-thread maximum recorded so far (4 in the whole integration set, 13 for fifteen console pages with their static files, 8 and 12 for eight and twelve overlapping heavy retrievals, 9 for the snapshot worker cases, 3 for the admission tests) equals the number of requests the test sent, no run combined console and heavy retrievals, and no run drove the concurrency to saturation or to a valve refusal at the default. Second, the qualification cells the plan of M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) named and the runs did not cover: the Serial collector; Parallel and generational Z at 2 GiB and 4 GiB; pressure after the response is committed; overshoot above the pressure threshold and headroom to the heap maximum at the shipped defaults; memory admission of a held summary (`mean_1`) pair at 2 GiB and 4 GiB, which needs a hold at the transport stage because the file read hold keeps 129 bytes per record against about 900 when the answer is built.

##### Scope

- A long-running measurement that sends console and heavy retrieval requests together and raises the concurrency step by step, recording busy threads, response times, memory decisions and valve refusals at each step, on 512 MiB and 2 GiB heaps, under the deployment `maxThreads` and under 40.
- The valve default decided from that table by a stated rule and recorded in the register, the book (installing page) and `TESTING.md`, with a test that runs at the chosen default rather than at a lowered threshold.
- Each qualification cell above measured and recorded, or recorded as not measured with the reason; the summary-pair admission needs a transport-stage hold in the test, for example a forwarding proxy that stops delivering the server's answer.

Out of scope: bounding the number of concurrent PVAccess calls (M8); changing the memory thresholds without a measurement that motivates it; the cause of the 31 MB baseline difference between 2026-10-22 and 2026-10-24.

##### Completion Criteria

- The measurement table exists with its evidence folder named, for two heaps and two `maxThreads` values.
- The valve default and the connector recommendation are recorded with their rule in the three documents, and the test at the default passes.
- Every qualification cell is measured or recorded as not measured with the reason.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- D8 (Decision Date 2026-10-24): the shipped defaults stay; the measurements move here from M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71).
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02 (D16). This row is part of release Ghost.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: draft
Plan Acceptance: none
Implementation Authorization: none
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

1. A load driver in the test code that mixes console page requests and heavy retrievals and raises the concurrency in steps, sampling `currentThreadsBusy`, response times and the console lines of the memory protection and the valve. Check: T1.
2. The rule and the chosen default, applied to the WAR declaration and the three documents, with the valve test run at the default. Check: T2.
3. The qualification cells, one run each, recorded in the Verification Results. Check: T3.

| T2 | Integration | `ValveConcurrencyTest` and the console check at the chosen default | same | The valve refuses above the default and admits below it; the console pages answer 200 below it |
| T3 | Integration, measurement | The qualification cells: Serial; Parallel and ZGC at 2 GiB and 4 GiB; pressure after commit; overshoot and headroom at the defaults; summary-pair admission at 2 GiB and 4 GiB with a transport-stage hold | same, with the collectors and heaps named | Each cell recorded with its result, or recorded as not measured with the reason |

#### M10 - Decide the stamping and filtering rule of summary bins at the window edges

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M17
Identity History: M17 to M10 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #59 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/59)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

`SummaryStatsCollectorEventStream` stamps the event of each bin at the bin midpoint and `MergeDedupConsumer.java:154` drops every event stamped after the requested end. When the bin is longer than the time between the request end and the last sample, the event of the last bin lies after the end and is dropped: a `mean_432000` request whose window ends inside the current five-day bin returns no event for that bin although its samples are inside the window (observed 2026-10-24 with `VariousPostProcessorTest`, 101 samples, HTTP 200 and an empty stream; the raw retrieval returned all 101). The test was corrected in 5efbd25b by ending its request at the next bin edge (M89 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71), issue #56); the product behaviour is unchanged and the rule has not been decided.

The midpoint stamp is not the stamp of the summary operators alone. `FillsCollectorEventStream` (`firstFill`, `lastFill`) stamps its bins with the same expression, and `LinearInterpolation` and `LoessInterpolation` extend `SummaryStatsPostProcessor` and so use the same collector, while `CAPlotBinning` stamps at the bin start and at quarter points of the bin and is not affected. The only place that drops an event stamped after the end is `MergeDedupConsumer.java:154`. The chart of the console (`browse/api.js:77`) and the viewer of the retrieval web application (`viewer.js:53`) ask for `meanSample_<seconds>(pv)` over a window whose end can be now, so the same bin may be missing at their right edge; this was read in the code and not observed. The analyzer page requests no data itself.
##### Scope

- Decide the rule for a bin whose samples lie inside the requested window when its midpoint stamp lies after the window end: keep the rule and state it in the book, return the bin at its midpoint, stamp it at the window end, or stamp it at the bin start; and whether the inherited first bin follows the same rule. The rule covers every operator that stamps its bins at the midpoint: the summary operators (the children of `SummaryStatsPostProcessor`, among them `mean`, `meanSample` and `median`), `firstFill`, `lastFill`, `linear` and `loess`.
- The consumers checked are the chart of the console (`browse/api.js`) and the viewer of the retrieval web application (`viewer.js`), by a real request through a local appliance, and the code examples of the book for MATLAB, Python and the Phoebus Data Browser.
- If the rule changes: the retrieval returns one event for such a bin regardless of the request time for every operator named above, the raw retrieval is unchanged, and `VariousPostProcessorTest` passes with its original one-day window again. If it stays: the retrieval page of the book states it.

Out of scope: the test window of `VariousPostProcessorTest` (M89 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71)); operators that stamp at the bin start or at quarter points (`caplotbinning`) and the raw retrieval; the source of clients outside this repository.

##### Completion Criteria

- Each operator named in the Scope is requested with an end inside a bin, and the result of each request is recorded.
- The decision is recorded with the consumers it was checked against.
- The product or the book reflects the decision as stated in Scope.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- D8 (Decision Date 2026-10-24): entered from M89 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71); the row stays Open until the owner decides the rule.
- Decided at the plan review (Decision Date 2026-11-03): the consumers are the chart of the console and the viewer of the retrieval web application (by a real request) and the code examples of the book, not clients outside the repository; the operators are all those that stamp at the midpoint. The rule itself is decided after the requests of T1 and the reading of T2 show the choices and their effects.
- Reading of the consumers (2026-11-03), for the owner's decision of the rule: the chart of the console (`bin = ceil(seconds / 600)`) and the viewer of the retrieval web application (`bin = ceil(seconds / 700)`) request `meanSample_<bin>` and plot every returned point at its stamp without clipping it to the window, so the dropped bin is a missing latest value of at most half a bin, a thousandth of the window or less (3 s of a 1 h window, 14 min of 7 days); the code examples of the book for MATLAB and Python print the events as returned, and the section of the Phoebus Data Browser only sets the URLs, so what these clients do with an event stamped after the window end is not in the repository and was not checked. At the start edge `MergeDedupConsumer` keeps the last event stamped at or before the start and returns it as the first event, so a bin stamped before the window is returned there while a bin stamped after the window end is dropped at the end; this was read in the code and not run.
- Decided after the reading (Decision Date 2026-11-03): the rule is to stamp the event of the bin that contains the window end at the window end when its midpoint lies after it (stamp = the earlier of the midpoint and the window end), for every operator named in the Scope, so that the event stays inside the window; the bins after that bin keep their midpoint stamp and are still dropped. `SummaryBinEdgeProbeTest` becomes the regression test of the rule. Read in the code for step 3, not yet run: the post-processor holds the request end (`SummaryStatsPostProcessor.end`), the two collectors (`SummaryStatsCollectorEventStream`, `FillsCollectorEventStream`) stamp at `binNum*intervalSecs + intervalSecs/2` and do not receive it, `MergeDedupConsumer` drops only events stamped after the end, so an event stamped at the end stays; an empty bin that contains the end and is filled with the previous value would now produce an event at the end, where `meanSample` produces none.
- Refinement made at the implementation (2026-11-03): the rule is applied to a bin that holds samples of its own; an empty bin that only repeats the value of the previous bin keeps its midpoint and is still dropped, so no new event appears at the window end for it. This replaces the effect expected at the decision for the operators that fill empty bins.
- Decided at the review of 2026-11-03 (owner confirmed): the collectors are shared with the PVAccess retrieval, whose consumer `PvaMergeDedupConsumer` does not drop events after the end, so over PVAccess the event of such a bin was returned with its midpoint after the end and is now stamped at the end; this was read in the code and is not checked by a test, and the issue itself showed only over HTTP. It is recorded in Closure Evidence as not measured.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: accepted
Plan Acceptance: 2026-11-03, after the plan review with two owner decisions (the consumers, the operators) and two third-person passes, the last with no finding above the floor; the acceptance covers steps 1 and 2, and step 3 waits for the rule that the owner decides after them
Implementation Authorization: 2026-11-03, owner approved implementing and verifying steps 1 and 2 of the accepted plan (the tests of the operators and the reading of the consumers) and, after deciding the rule on the same day, step 3 (stamp the event of the bin that contains the window end at the window end); no commit or push is authorized by it
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

1. T1 first: a test of its own in the manner of `VariousPostProcessorTest` (the same archive of 101 samples through the real retrieval; the existing test is not extended) requests `mean`, `meanSample`, `firstFill`, `lastFill`, `linear` and `loess` with an end inside the current bin and records which return no event, then `meanSample_<seconds>` is requested as the chart of the console does on a local appliance. Check: T1.
2. Read the chart of the console, the viewer of the retrieval web application and the code examples of the book against the four rules, and present the choices with what each changes for those consumers; the owner decides. Check: T2.
3. Apply the decided rule to the product or the book. Check: T3.

#### M11 - Check the retrieval operators outside the declared-size check

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M20
Identity History: M20 to M11 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #60 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/60)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

The audit of the retrieval protection (M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) / T2, 2026-10-21) classified candidates as hypotheses with a next check; the fill operators of its `Event per bin` row were checked and corrected (a27f260a), and the others neither ran nor were carried to a later row until D10; the fifth candidate below, the PVAccess name list, comes from the sweep of 2026-10-24. The declared-size estimate of `RetrievalMemoryProtection.Request.admit` covers `Optimized`, `OptimizedWithLastSample`, the `SummaryStatsPostProcessor` subclasses, `CAPlotBinning`, `FirstFill`, `LastFill` and `LastSample`; the in-flight check runs where `checkCurrent` is called (`MergeDedupConsumer`, `PvaMergeDedupConsumer`, `SummaryStatsCollectorEventStream`, `FillsCollectorEventStream`, `CAPlotBinning`, `OptimizedWithLastSample`, `GetDataAtTime`, and `DataRetrievalServlet` once before and once after it handles a request, lines 276 to 310). Outside both, read in the code on 2026-10-24 and not run: `FirstSamplePP`, `DeadBand`, `Fliers` and `IgnoreFliers` copy the events of their source streams into an `ArrayListEventStream` inside their own `wrap` loops (`FirstSamplePP.java:64-77`, `DeadBand.java:72-130`, `Fliers.java:66-76`, `IgnoreFliers.java:75-85`); `Median`, `Kurtosis` and `Skewness` keep every value of a bin in a `DescriptiveStatistics` while a scalar summary bin is counted at `scalarBinBytes`, 112 bytes by default; `getDataForPVs` (`DataRetrievalServlet.java:756`), `getDataAtTime`, `getDataAtTimeForAppliance` and the PVAccess call (`PvaGetPVData.java:452`) take a list of names with no limit found, while the declared sizes of one request add up in its single budget and a raw read declares nothing. `Nth` and `NCount` are bounded by `MAX_COUNT` (`Nth.java:31`), recorded as CD-18. The run of 2026-11-02 (T1) corrected one premise: the source streams of the four operators that copy events are wrapped by `RetrievalMemoryProtection.guard` in the constructor of `CallableEventStream`, so the in-loop check reads their events every 1,024 events; the refusal of `ignoreflyers` came through `GuardedStream.next` in `IgnoreFliers.java:77`. The declared-size estimate still does not count them.

##### Scope

- Run each candidate as a real request through a built appliance at a 512 MiB heap, started through `RetrievalMemoryNetworkFixture` (heap from `archappl.memory.test.heap`, default 512m; a generated 1 Hz store of `MemoryQualification:SineV1` over 30 days; the Memory admission tests paragraph of `TESTING.md` describes it): `median_3600` over 30 days next to `mean_3600`; `firstSample`, `deadBand`, `flyers` and `ignoreflyers` over the raw range of one PV (the operator names are case sensitive, and a name that matches no operator is refused as unknown, which is not a result for the candidate); `getDataForPVs` by POST with a JSON body (`Content-Type: application/json`, an array of names) of 10,000 names, which `getPVNamesFromPostBody` reads from the body; the type is sent exactly as `application/json`, without a charset, because the server compares the whole string and reads any other type as plain text, one name per line, so that a JSON array on one line becomes a single name that matches nothing and the result would not be a result for the candidate; and by GET with the most names that the address allows (the request line of the container is limited to 8 KiB by `maxHttpHeaderSize`, and request parameters beyond `maxParameterCount`, default 10,000, are ignored), a limit that is measured first and recorded; `getDataAtTime` with a list of 10,000 names; the PVAccess call with a list of 10,000 names. Record the status, the memory after collection and whether the JVM stayed alive.
- For a candidate that exhausts the heap or ends the JVM, extend the declared-size estimate or add the in-loop check at the place named above, with a test that fails without it; for one that is refused or completes safely, record it as examined in `docs/CLOSED_DOORS.md`, after the owner decides its fate (D10): a section of its own gives the examined commit and the date of the decision, and one row for each such candidate gives the Candidate, the Verdict, the Premise and the Evidence, in the way of CD-18.

Out of scope: the thread and valve limits and the other qualification cells (M9); bounding the concurrent PVAccess calls (M8); a change of the memory thresholds without a measurement that motivates it.

##### Completion Criteria

- Each of the five candidates has a recorded real-request result at 512 MiB: refused, meaning HTTP 400 or 503 with the name of the condition in the response; or completed, meaning HTTP 200 with the whole answer and the JVM still running; or exhausted, meaning an `OutOfMemoryError` in the console log or the JVM ended. A refusal for an operator name that matches no operator is not a result. For the PVAccess call there is no HTTP status: refused means the retrieval log names the condition and the client ends at its own timeout, and completed means the whole answer arrives.
- Every candidate that exhausted the heap has a correction with a test that fails without it and passes with it.
- Each candidate that is refused or completes safely, and that the owner decides to keep, is recorded in `docs/CLOSED_DOORS.md` as examined, in the way of CD-18.
- The retrieval page of the book names the operators that declare no size, if any remain.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- Decision Date: 2026-11-02. The owner decided to record the results of T1 as they stand and to run more tests later; the input of T1 is one PV over 30 days.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02. The owner decided to keep the five candidates as they are and to close them in `docs/CLOSED_DOORS.md` (CD-19 to CD-23, in the section "retrieval operators and name lists outside the declared-size check - real requests at 512 MiB").
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02. The owner decided that the request harness is an integration test class kept in `src/test`, in the way of `RetrievalMemoryNetworkTest`, and not a driver used once.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02. The owner decided the sizes of the three name lists: 10,000 names for each of them. `getDataForPVs` accepts its names in the body of a POST and in the address of a GET; the 10,000 names are sent in the body, because the address of a GET cannot carry them (about 8 KiB), and the GET is also run with the most names that the address allows, so that a result at a size the servlet did not see is not recorded as safe.
- D10 (Decision Date 2026-10-24): the five candidates enter as long-term work and are not checked before the merge to master. D15 (Decision Date 2026-10-31) supersedes this for this row: M11 is part of release Ahsoka, so the candidates are checked before that merge.

#### M15 - Archiving continues across a change of the record

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M10
Identity History: M10 to M15 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #48 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/48)
Status: Deferred

##### Summary

M73 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) observed, on 2026-10-17 and again on 2026-10-18 on the one IOC and the one appliance of D3, that the engine detects a change of the archived record but stops at counting: a record that posts faster than the rate estimated at the archive request loses most events to a sample buffer of 11 (T1); a changed unit, precision or limit reaches the archiver only through the property subscription or, for the unit and the precision, at a reconnect, and the display limits are never read again (T2); a record that comes back under the same name with another type has every event dropped and counted while the type information keeps the old type (T3, T5); the way out is manual, pause and `changeTypeForPV` and resume, converts the history (a double 0.0057 becomes the integer 0) and refuses a string or a waveform with HTTP 500 (T4). The role of the archiver is the opposite: to keep the signal without a gap (D4).

##### Scope

- The engine detects each change that M73 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) names and goes on storing under the new condition from the instant of the change, on its own: a changed type opens a new type from that instant, a faster record gets a buffer and a sampling that follow it, changed metadata is read again.
- The earlier samples stay readable as they were stored: a PV has a type boundary on its time axis, and the stores and the retrieval know it, because a PB file holds one type.
- The console and the manual show the boundary and the change.

Out of scope until the plan is written: the exact store layout of the boundary; the policy for a change the engine cannot follow.

##### Completion Criteria

- The five checks of M73 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71), run again, store the signal through each change without a manual step, and a window of `getData` across the change returns the earlier samples with their stored type and the later samples with the new one.
- No event is dropped for a reason the engine could have followed; what is dropped is logged with its reason.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- M73 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) is the observation this row answers; D4 defers the row to the next major release (Decision Date: 2026-10-17) and limits this release to M77 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71).
- Transition: Deferred on 2026-10-17 by D4; it returns to Not started when the next major release opens.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. The owner decided that the reliability of the buffer and its adaptation stay in this row, as long-term work that follows release Ahsoka; release Ahsoka takes only the review of the sampling policies, as M30 (D13).
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02 (D16). This row is part of release Ghost.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: draft
Plan Acceptance: none
Implementation Authorization: none
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

1. Design of the type boundary in the stores and the retrieval, with the data-change rules of the PB files.
2. The engine reactions, one per change of M73 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71).
3. The console and the manual.

Buffer adaptation ideas for plan evaluation, recorded on 2026-10-29; these do not select a design or authorize implementation:

- The operator reports that changing a MONITOR period from 1.0 s to 0.1 s stopped the observed buffer loss. This is operator evidence, not an independently measured before-and-after result; the observation duration and counter continuity were not recorded. `ArchiveEngine.computeBufferCapacity` derives capacity from the configured period, while `SampleBuffer` holds a final capacity and drops the oldest event when full.
- Prefer evaluating automatic buffer capacity adjustment independently of the configured sampling method and period. MONITOR should continue receiving its subscribed events; SCAN timing and operator settings must not silently change to resolve a capacity shortage.
- Measure incoming valid events before overflow handling, buffer occupancy and actual buffer-swap/write delay over bounded windows. Include events involved in overflow in the incoming-rate measure; `ArchiveChannel` increments its existing event count only when `buffer.add` reports no overflow, so that counter alone understates input during loss. Separate a faster source or burst from a persistently slow or failing storage writer.
- Estimate capacity from observed input rate, the write interval and a bounded allowance for bursts and write delay. Evaluate growth before overflow, with a rapid response to bursts; choose thresholds, observation windows and growth limits from measurements. A periodic check alone cannot guarantee preservation of an abrupt burst before it reacts.
- Enforce both per-PV and appliance-wide memory budgets, accounting for waveform payloads, the current buffer, a pending write and temporary memory during resizing. Preserve queued events and their order while coordinating with callbacks and buffer swaps; evaluate a resize path that does not recreate the CA/PVA connection. Do not discard an unacknowledged write batch to make room.
- Add a minimum interval between adjustments and a separate sustained-low-load condition before any shrink. At the memory limit or during persistent storage failure, stop growth and report the cause, capacity, occupancy and loss counters; automatic growth cannot repair inadequate sustained write throughput or recover previously dropped samples.
- Show each automatic adjustment and its reason in Operations and PV Details. Define an enable/disable policy, manual override precedence and whether learned capacity survives restart; these remain planning decisions.

Findings of 2026-10-31 from the verification of the sampling explanation, observed on the real path (packaged WARs, a soft IOC, a headless browser; the drivers and logs are in the untracked `work/m24-repro` of the workstation that holds this checkout). They are evidence for the same evaluation and do not select a design:

- A Channel Access PV that posts twice per second, archived MONITOR with a period of 1.0 s (a buffer of 11 slots), lost samples in every write interval: five runs counted 62 to 64 buffer-full losses, and the retrieved samples form runs of at most 11 consecutive values with a gap before each run, so the newest 11 of an interval are kept and the earlier ones are missing. A real `SampleBuffer` of 11 slots given 20 samples kept the newest 11 and counted 9 losses.
- On that PV the Details row "Estimated event rate (events/sec)" read 1.14 while the PV posted 2.0 per second, which confirms on the real path that the counter understates the input during loss; it tends to the capacity divided by the write period. No console text can name the real input rate of a PV that loses samples, so the help tells the operator to take it from the record.
- The shipped `src/sitespecific/default/classpathfiles/policies.py` gives every policy, including `2HzPVs`, which applies above 2 events per second, a MONITOR period of 1.0 s and therefore 11 slots; a PV above about 1.1 events per second loses samples under it unless a site policy or the operator sets a shorter period. The `BPMS` policy raises the period to reduce the stored rate, but the period of a MONITOR PV does not limit what is stored (every change was stored in the runs above) and only shrinks the buffer. A site policy may differ.
- The Operations page advised lengthening the period of a fast PV to slow it, which for a MONITOR PV only shrinks its buffer; the row of the sampling explanation corrected that advice. The dialog text, the help section, the hint of the Archive page and the cards of the Operations page describe the buffer as built today and are revised with any change of its sizing.
- The owner's assessment on 2026-10-31 is that the stored signal is not reliably complete under the present sizing and that the capacity, the policy default and the counters are to be settled together by this evaluation.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T1 | Engine and stores | The five checks of M73 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) (`work/exp/m73/run_all.sh`) on the one IOC and the one appliance of D3, read for continuity instead of for loss | Packaged WARs, the IOC of D3 | Every change is followed; a window across the change holds both sides |
| T2 | Engine and storage integration | After plan acceptance, drive the shipped engine with independently counted sequenced input: steady rate increases, abrupt bursts, delayed or failed writes, recovery, many PVs and waveform payloads; compare stored sequence, timestamps and values, memory use, adjustment records and connection continuity | Isolated appliance, real CA/PVA source and actual storage writer; resource limits fixed by the accepted plan | Within the declared load and memory envelope, adaptation preserves queued samples without duplicates or changed sampling settings; outside that envelope, bounded memory and explicit loss or write-failure reporting; no claim of zero loss outside the measured scope |

##### GitHub Projection

Title: Archiving continues across a change of the record

Body: The engine detects a change of an archived record but stops at counting, so a change on the IOC side ends the recording of the signal until an operator acts. Observed on a local appliance with a soft IOC: a record that posts faster than the rate estimated at the archive request loses most events to a sample buffer of 11 (58 samples and 445 lost in 46 s at 10 Hz); a changed unit, precision or limit reaches the archiver only through the property subscription or at a reconnect, and the display limits are never read again; a record that comes back under the same name with another type has every event dropped and counted while the type information keeps the old type; the way out, pause, `changeTypeForPV`, resume, converts the history (a double 0.0057 becomes the integer 0) and answers HTTP 500 for a string or a waveform (`UnsupportedOperationException` in `ThruNumberAndStringConversion`). The archiver should keep the signal without a gap: detect the change and go on storing under the new condition from the instant of the change, with the earlier samples readable as they were stored, which needs a type boundary on the time axis of a PV that the stores and the retrieval know. Planned for the next major release; in the current release the engine logs the change and pauses a PV whose type changed.

#### M16 - Modernize the retained state-changing sample scripts

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M1
Identity History: M1 to M16 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #63 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/63)
Status: Not started

##### Summary

M37 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) originally held fourteen retained sample scripts. On 2026-10-04 the owner kept the read-only and alert groups in M37 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) and moved the two groups that change appliance state here: recovery selection (abortNeverConnectedPVs.py, resumePausedPVsMatchingPattern.py) and storage and configuration (consolidatePausedPVs.py, consolidateArchivedData.py, changeArchiveStore.py, addPostProcessingOperator.py, removeMetaFields.py). M37 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71)'s Summary records their current defects: no timeouts, HTTP 200 taken as success, the unchecked pause, consolidate and resume sequence with an unconditional resume, the full PVTypeInfo post with override=true, and the getAllPVs default limit of 500.

##### Scope

Bring these seven scripts to the contract M37 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) defines for its scripts (explicit BPL URL, per-request `--timeout`, exit 0, 1 and 2, results on stdout and diagnostics on stderr), reusing M37 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71)'s shared Bash mechanics, while keeping each script's target selection, request order, side effects and final state explicit. Document each in the scripting page.

Out of scope: the M37 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) scripts; the eight commands delivered by M17 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71); server corrections, which take their own scope when a script exposes a defect.

##### Completion Criteria

- Each of the seven scripts has a defined input, response, dependency, timeout and failure contract, reports an application-level rejection and an uncertain outcome as such, and does not report success on HTTP 200 alone.
- Each keeps the procedure recorded in M17 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71)'s function map, and every intentional change from the legacy behavior is named with its reason.
- Each is verified by executing its shipped entry point against the real appliance and the shipped IOC fixture, with the original and the replacement compared on the same fixtures before the original is removed; the scripting page documents each.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- Origin: split from M37 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) by the owner on 2026-10-04; unassigned until the owner assigns it.
- M37 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71): the language (Bash), the per-request timeout and the shared mechanics come from M37 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71).

#### M17 - Mail delivery for the alert checks

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M2
Identity History: M2 to M17 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #64 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/64)
Status: Not started

##### Summary

The three alert scripts (checkConnectedPVs.py, checkTypeChangedPVs.py, storageSizeCheck.py) send their alerts by mail through the emailHandler.py helper. On 2026-10-04 the owner made them on-demand checks in M37 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) that report by stdout and exit status and send no mail, and moved emailHandler.py and mail delivery here. Today the helper reads its JSON configuration (named by `ARCHAPPL_NAGIOS_EMAIL_CONFIG`, default `/arch/tools/config/archappl_email_config`) at import, before the calling script parses its arguments; opens its SMTP connection without a timeout; and names only the first recipient in the To header while sending to all.

Provide mail delivery for the alert checks as an option on top of their stdout and exit-status contract: configuration read after argument parsing, a per-connection timeout, credentials kept off the command line, and a defined failure status.

Out of scope: the alert checks themselves (M37 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71)).

- Decision carried from M37 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) (Decision Date 2026-10-04): emailHandler.py reads its configuration after the calling script has parsed its arguments.

#### M18 - Verify the proxy to an external Archiver Appliance

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M3
Identity History: M3 to M18 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #65 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/65)
Status: Not started

##### Summary

An appliance can register another EPICS Archiver Appliance as an external data server (`addExternalArchiverServer` and `addExternalArchiverServerArchives` with the archive `pbraw`, server type `ARCHAPPL_PBRAW` on the Integration page). For a PV it does not archive, retrieval asks each registered appliance `areWeArchivingPV` and, on a positive answer, reads the data from that appliance's `getData.raw` through the `pbraw://` plugin and returns it (`DataRetrievalServlet.checkIfPVisServedByExternalServer`); `GetMatchingPVs` and `GetDataAtTime` also consult the registered servers. No test calls the registration calls or exercises this decision path. The failover tests (`common/Failover*Test.java`) read another appliance through the same `pbraw://` plugin, but through a `merge://` data store set on one PV, not through a registered external server.

##### Scope

Establish by a real run whether the proxy to an external Archiver Appliance works on this platform, and keep the run as an integration test.

Out of scope: the Channel Archiver XML-RPC data server (`CA_XMLRPC`); the interface for registering servers; corrections, which take their own scope when the run exposes a defect.

##### Completion Criteria

- An integration test starts two appliances, archives a PV from the shipped IOC fixture on the second only, registers the second as an external server of the first through the mgmt calls, and observes through the first appliance: the PV's data from `getData`, the PV in the name match, and its value from the value-at-time call, each matching the second appliance's own answer.
- The test also observes that removing the server (`removeExternalArchiverServer`) ends the proxy, and that a PV neither appliance archives returns no data.
- The outcome is recorded: the proxy works as described, or the defect found is recorded as separate work for owner direction.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- Origin: recorded on 2026-10-12 at the owner's direction during the review of the management interface, where the Integration page keeps only the external Archiver Appliance servers.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02 (D16). This row is part of release Ghost.
