#### M19 - Verify signal storage and important-signal delivery under client overload

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M15
Identity History: M15 to M19 on 2026-10-26
GitHub Issue: #55 - https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/55
Status: Deferred

##### Summary

Verify appliance behavior under excessive client requests according to the operational priorities: first, signal storage; second, storage and delivery of important signals; last, client request handling. Rejecting or stopping client requests is acceptable when required to preserve the higher-priority functions. Process survival and memory thresholds alone do not establish this behavior.

##### Scope

- Combine real IOC updates, actual archiving and delivery, and concurrent HTTP/PVA retrieval load in an isolated appliance using the intended deployment layout.
- Compare emitted sequence numbers, values and timestamps with stored records and destination receipts before, during and after overload; account explicitly for configured sampling and deadband.
- Cover oversized requests, concurrent accumulation, in-flight memory growth, slow or disconnected clients, repeated retries and repeated overload/recovery cycles.
- Retain memory, GC, worker and connection observations to explain the storage, delivery, rejection and recovery results. Keep controlled-exposure checks separate from operation without test-driven GC or filesystem read holds.

Out of scope: immediate implementation or execution; changing M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) / T6, T7 or T8 completion criteria; selecting a new collector, admission policy or signal-priority mechanism without a separate accepted plan.

##### Completion Criteria

- An accepted contract defines important signals, delivery destinations and protocols, expected stored/delivered records, allowed delays and client rejection/recovery behavior.
- Real combined-load tests demonstrate the required storage and important-signal delivery throughout overload, with no unexplained missing, duplicated or corrupted records against that contract.
- Excessive requests are refused or stopped with observable failure, and request handling recovers within the agreed bound without restarting the appliance; failures and missing exposure remain explicit.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- Decision Date: 2026-10-22. Retain this work as a long-term Backlog plan; exclude it from current execution. Existing M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) scope and completion criteria remain unchanged.
- Priority order: signal storage; important-signal storage and delivery; client requests.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31 (D14). The owner decided that this row is not part of release Ahsoka. It is the higher-level requirement; the sample buffer work that M30 does for the policies in the release and M15 does after it implements one part of it, and what M30 measures (buffer losses against event rates on the real path) is an input for the plan of this row. The row stays a long-term plan.
- Related evidence: M85 (previous document, commit bfb0dc71) request-protection measurements and M8 concurrency planning. No execution dependency is assigned by this record.
- Open planning details: important-signal selection, delivery semantics, allowable storage/delivery latency, client behavior during overload, deployment resources and observation duration. These are future plan inputs, not permission to implement.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02 (D16). This row is part of release Ghost.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: draft
Plan Acceptance: none
Implementation Authorization: none
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

1. Define the operational contract and record a normal-load baseline with independently observed IOC emissions, stored records and delivery receipts. Check: T1.
2. Combine continuous signal production with the overload scenarios and normal client probes, judging storage and important-signal delivery before request service. Check: T2.
3. Repeat overload/recovery cycles and the selected heap profiles; verify final data integrity and resource recovery. Check: T3.

##### Test Plan

| Label | Layer | Method | Environment | Expected Result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T1 | Operational baseline | Observe real IOC emissions, stored records, important-signal delivery receipts and normal client responses | Isolated appliance; resources and signal contract to be defined | Actual records and delays satisfy the accepted contract before overload |
| T2 | Combined-load integration | Continue T1 production while applying oversized, concurrent, growing, slow/disconnected and retrying HTTP/PVA requests | Same appliance and signal contract; no substituted internal request path | Storage and important-signal delivery meet their higher-priority requirements; client requests complete correctly or fail observably |
| T3 | Repeated recovery | Repeat normal/overload/recovery cycles and selected heap profiles; compare final records and worker/connection state | Same deployed code; duration and limits to be defined | Data integrity and agreed recovery bounds hold without restart or accumulating abandoned work |

#### M22 - Restore console pages after tab navigation

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M22
Identity History: none
GitHub Issue: #72 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/72)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

The reported viewer behavior loses the previously viewed PVs when the operator changes tabs. A measurement of the console in a browser on 2026-11-01 shows where the loss comes from: the Back button restores every page, because the browser keeps the page and the pages do not block it, while a click on a menu entry is a new visit that opens the page with an empty address, so the selected PVs of the viewer, the names typed in Archive and the inputs of Retrieval are lost. The row therefore uses what the browser already provides: the state of a page in its own address, the menu returning to the last address of a page, and the input memory of the browser.

##### Scope

- Make a click on a menu entry open the page at the last address that the page had in this browser tab, which restores the selected and plotted PVs of the viewer (the viewer already writes its state to its address) and keeps the existing PV limits and address behavior. The browser tab keeps only that address, in `sessionStorage`, and the menu entries get their `href` set to it when the page loads, so that the browser keeps following the link itself (a handler that opens the address by script instead of letting the browser follow the link breaks opening in a new tab and the middle button); an explicit address of the link wins over the kept one. The address is recorded when the state changes and on `pagehide`, never in an `unload` or `beforeunload` handler, which would remove the page from the back/forward cache and break the Back restoration that this row keeps.
- Remove `autocomplete="off"` from the six places that carry it: `#q` of Browse PVs, the text area `#names` and the input `#controlling` of Archive, `#measurePV` and `#dataPV` of Retrieval, and the form `#addForm` of the data viewer, so that the browser's input memory and restoration work.
- Offer the names of archived PVs as suggestions through a `<datalist>` on the single-line inputs `#controlling`, `#measurePV` and `#dataPV`, in the way the data viewer already does for its `#pvName`: from two typed characters, after 200 ms, the 20 names that `getMatchingPVs` returns for `*text*`. A text area has no `list` attribute, so `#names` keeps only the browser's input memory. The Contains, Glob and Regex modes of Find and their results do not change.
- Carry the inputs that the address does not carry into the address of Archive, Retrieval and External, one page after the other; the names of Archive go into its address up to 100, as Browse limits its PVs, and a note says when more are typed. The data viewer is served by the retrieval web application on its own port, which is another origin than the console and has its own `sessionStorage`; the Analyzer entry of the menu keeps passing its address to the viewer, the viewer keeps its Back restoration, and its own last address is outside this row.
- Keep deliberate PV removal and clearing effective when the operator returns.
- Say the new behavior where the operator reads about it: one sentence of the item "The address" of `help.html` and one of the page of Browse PVs in `docs/book/src/interface.md` that say that a menu entry opens a page at the last address it had in this browser tab, that a new tab starts fresh, and that an explicit address of a link wins; the same text names the documented precedence of the completion criteria.

Out of scope: changing acquisition, sampling or archived data; implementing the analyzer phases; a general browsing-history service or cross-device synchronization; a framework or library for state; ranked approximate search and bulk selection, which M23 keeps.

##### Completion Criteria

- After selecting and plotting PVs, navigating to another tab and returning restores the same PV names and plotted subset without manual re-entry.
- Repeated navigation introduces no duplicate PVs, and removed or cleared PVs do not reappear from saved state.
- Explicit addresses continue to open their requested PVs according to the documented precedence; missing or unusable saved state leaves the viewer usable.
- A click on the menu entry of Browse PVs after visiting another page opens the viewer at its last address, and the Back button keeps restoring every page as it does now.
- The six places no longer carry `autocomplete="off"`, the single-line PV name inputs suggest at most 20 names after two typed characters without altering the results of the three Find modes, and typing stays responsive.
- The inputs of Archive, Retrieval and External return through the menu as well as through the Back button, the names of Archive up to 100, and a longer list is cut with a note instead of being lost silently.
- The help page and the book say how a menu entry opens a page, that a new tab starts fresh and that an explicit address wins.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- Decision Date: 2026-10-29. Record PV selection persistence as a separate work item; no implementation is authorized by this entry.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The owner decided to use the browser's standard means instead of a new mechanism: the state of a page in its address, and the input memory of the browser through `autocomplete`, with name suggestions through `<datalist>` done in this row together with it.
- Measurement of 2026-11-01 01:08 PDT on the changed tree: headless Firefox 153.6.0 ESR against an appliance built from commit 25671994c764bacbe3518c31390f0f847368e341, one soft IOC, one archived Channel Access PV. The pages send no `no-store` header and no script of the console handles `unload`. With the Back button, Browse PVs kept its address, its selected row and the state of the page, Archive kept two typed names with the method SCAN and the period 2, and Retrieval kept two typed PV names; the state of the page itself had survived (`navigation` type `back_forward` and a marker set before leaving still present). After a click on the menu entry of the same page the address was empty, no row was present in Browse PVs and the inputs of Archive were empty. Not measured: other browsers, and a return when the browser no longer holds the page.
- Sources of the standard means (read on 2026-11-01): back/forward cache (web.dev), the History API (MDN), the Navigation API (MDN, Baseline since 2026-01), `sessionStorage` and `<datalist>`; the `autocomplete="off"` attribute is known to switch off the restoration of form values on Back, with browser-specific behavior. The matching rules of `<datalist>` (prefix, substring, case) were not found in an authoritative page and must be measured.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The owner directed that the findings of the first third-person review of this plan be applied. Facts checked by that review: a text area has no `list` property in Firefox (`hasListProperty` false, true for the input types), so `#names` cannot take a `<datalist>`; the data viewer already fills a `<datalist>` for `#pvName` from two typed characters with a delay of 200 ms and a limit of 20 names, and `LIMITS.indexed` of `browse.js` is 500,000 names, which cannot become options; the form `#addForm` of `archViewer.html` carries `autocomplete="off"` as a sixth place; the viewer is on the retrieval web application's port, another origin than the console (the forwarding page `analyzer-forward.js` uses `dataRetrievalURL`); an `unload` handler removes a page from the back/forward cache on desktop Chrome and Firefox (web.dev). The title and the body of issue #72 still describe only the restoration of the viewer, and #73 the earlier shared scope, so before this row is closed their bodies are replaced with the projection of this detail, through the issue operation.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The owner left the choice for `#q` of Find to a test, with the author's choice tried first: `#q` gets no `<datalist>` and loses `autocomplete="off"`, because the Find list already shows the matches as the text is typed, the Contains mode takes several words in any order (a `<datalist>` matches only text that is part of a name) and the query is already in the address. Test of 2026-11-01 08:03 PDT: a copy of the mgmt web application held outside the repository with copies of three pages without `autocomplete="off"`, headless Firefox 153.6.0 ESR with the page cache switched off (`browser.sessionhistory.max_total_viewers` 0, so the browser does not hold the page), one appliance and one soft IOC. With the Back button, `#q` kept the same value in the shipped page and in the copy, equal to the `q` of the address; Archive lost its two names and its controlling PV in the shipped page while its method and period were restored, and restored all four inputs in the copy with its summary equal to its inputs; Retrieval lost its two PV names in the shipped page and restored both in the copy. No inconsistency between an input and the state of its page appeared in the copy. Not measured: other browsers, the saving of typed values for suggestions, and the matching rules of a `<datalist>`. The drivers and logs are in the untracked `work/m24-repro` (`verify10.py`, `verify10.log`) of the workstation that holds this checkout.
- Prototype of step 2, run on 2026-11-01 08:09 PDT in a copy of the mgmt web application held outside the repository, in which `js/menu.js` sets the `href` of each menu entry to the last address that its page had in this browser tab (read from `sessionStorage` when the page loads) and records the address of the current page on `pagehide`, with headless Firefox 153.6.0 ESR against the appliance of commit 25671994c764bacbe3518c31390f0f847368e341. On a first visit the menu kept its plain addresses. After Browse PVs had written `#q=bpm+03&pv=...` to its address and the page was left through the menu, the entry Browse PVs on Archive carried that address, a click on it opened Browse PVs with the same query, the selected row and the entry still marked as the current page. After the address of Browse PVs was cleared and the page left again, the entry returned to the plain `index.html` and opened an empty viewer. `sessionStorage` held one key per page. Links that are not menu entries were not changed. Not measured: other browsers, a page opened in a new tab, and the pages other than Browse PVs and Archive. The driver and log are in the untracked `work/m24-repro` (`verify11.py`, `verify11.log`).
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The owner directed that the findings of the first second-person review of this plan be applied: the menu entries get their `href` set when the page loads, so that the browser keeps following the link itself (a handler that opens the address by script would break opening in a new tab and the middle button); step 2 covers every menu entry and every page at once and step 4 only carries inputs into addresses; the suggestions are counted from the `<option>` elements and the shown list is checked by hand, because the list of the browser is not part of the page; the names of Archive go into its address up to 100, as Browse limits its PVs, because the page has no limit of its own and a long list would make a long address.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The owner directed that the findings of the second second-person review and of the third third-person review of this plan be applied: the behavior of the menu, the new tab and the precedence of an explicit address are documented in `help.html` and in the book, because the completion criteria refer to a documented precedence and no text stated it; and the reason for the `href` replacement is that a handler which opens the address by script breaks opening in a new tab, not every click handler.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The suggestions use `getAllPVs` of the console with the glob `*text*` and limit 20 (`js/suggest.js`), because the console has no `getMatchingPVs`; the data viewer keeps its own call.
- M23 keeps the evaluation of ranked approximate matches and the selection of all matching PVs; the native name suggestions through `<datalist>` belong to this row. No execution dependency is established. M1 also concerns viewer address state; coordinate any shared paths when planning without making this item depend on the analyzer redesign.

This one change covers every menu entry and every page at once, and the step is done when T1 is measured on the entries of Browse PVs and Archive. Checks: T1, T2.

Remove `autocomplete="off"` from the six places and add the bounded `<datalist>` of the data viewer's pattern to the three single-line inputs of Archive and Retrieval; leave the results of the Find modes unchanged. The number of suggestions is read from the `<option>` elements of the `<datalist>`, and whether the browser shows its list is checked by hand in a desktop browser. Check: T3.
4. Put the inputs that the address of Archive, Retrieval and External does not carry into it, one page after the other, with the names of Archive limited to 100 in the address and a note when more are typed, and measure each page with the Back button and the menu. Checks: T1, T2, T4.
5. Write the sentences of the scope into `help.html` and `docs/book/src/interface.md`, and build the book. Check: T5.

#### M23 - Select matches and suggest similar names in Browse PVs

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M23
Identity History: none
GitHub Issue: #73 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/73)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

After narrowing the viewer's Find results by a prefix and Glob pattern, the operator needs one action to select the matching PVs. The search should also provide more useful assistance when the exact name is not known.

##### Scope

- One control of the list tools of Browse PVs, "Select matches", that adds the PVs of the exact result list (Contains, Glob or Regex, in list order) to the selected PVs of the table: the PVs already selected stay, a name already selected is skipped, and the control stops at the limit of the table. It says in the note of the table how many were added and how many matches were left out ("Added 63 of 412 matches; the table holds at most N PVs"). When the list came from the server and is partial (the count line reads "or more"), the control uses the names the list holds and says that the list is partial. Matches that cannot be added do not remove or replace a selected PV. The control is off while "Selected only" is on, because the list then shows the selected PVs and not the matches.
- Measure the cost of the selected PVs on the real page (the status read, the value and type requests, the drawing of the table, and the actions on the whole table) at 100, 300 and 1,000 selected PVs, and set the limit of the table (`LIMITS.table`) from the measurement. The limit of the plotted PVs (`LIMITS.chart`, 5) does not change. The address of the analyzer (the link "Open all selected in the analyzer" and the viewer it forwards to) carries the selected names in its query, and the server refuses an address of about 8 KB or more (measured on 2026-11-02 with names of the form `SR:BPM:NNN:X`: 350 names answered 200 and 400 names answered HTTP 400, for the viewer and for `analyzer.html`); the link therefore carries the first selected PVs that fit in a query of 6 KB, and when it cuts it says so in the visible note of the table ("The analyzer link carries the first N PVs") and in its title, and the measurement opens the link at each size. The address keeps the selected names up to the same limit; the measurement includes opening an address with that many names. The help page and the book say the new limit.
- Compare, on a list of 500,000 PV names, the exact Worker search of Browse with lightweight ways to rank and find approximate matches, and record time per query, memory and the quality of the answers (done, see T3): uFuzzy, fuzzysort, fzf and microfuzz, and a ranking by the structure of the name that needs no library. The exact modes keep their meaning.
- Use uFuzzy 1.0.19 (MIT, one file of 8.4 KB, no index) for a separate list "Similar names" under the list of matches. The names Worker runs it only for a Contains search of at least three characters that has fewer than 10 exact matches, with its typo setting (`intraMode` 1) and with its setting for missing characters (`intraIns` 1), and gives at most 20 names that are not among the exact matches: up to 10 names of the typo setting first, in the order uFuzzy gives them, then up to 10 names that only the setting for missing characters found, in the order uFuzzy gives them, so that a name that is one character short is not hidden by ten names of the other kind. The list is shown only when no prefix button and no Show report is chosen and the Worker holds the list of names (when the server answers the search because the names exceed the index limit, there is no list to search and none is shown), and only then; a click on a name selects it as a click in the list does; "Select matches" and the count of matches never use it; the Glob and Regex modes show none. The library is placed in `staticcontent/js/vendor/` with a record of its source and its SHA-256 and its license, in the way of the chart library, and the interface static checks verify the file.
- Revision of 2026-11-02: the names Worker offers no similar names when the text of the search, trimmed and compared without regard to case, is equal to the name of one of the exact matches, because the operator then holds the name and the list only repeats it with its neighbours. A text that is a part of one or more names (for example `SR:C01:BPM1`) keeps the rule above. The table limit stays 100 and is not made a setting.

Out of scope: the native name suggestions of the PV name inputs through `<datalist>` and the input memory of the browser, which M22 did; changing acquisition or archived data; automatically plotting every selected PV; changing the limit of five plotted PVs; a search library other than uFuzzy, a ranking of the exact matches, an approximate search for the Glob and Regex modes, and a setting that lets a reader raise the limit of the table (decided on 2026-11-02 not to be built).

##### Completion Criteria

- "Select matches" adds the matching PVs together, is off while "Selected only" is on, without duplicates and without removing a selected PV, stops at the limit, and says how many were added and how many were left out.
- The measurement of the cost of 100, 300 and 1,000 selected PVs is recorded, and the limit of the table is the value that the owner sets from the record; an address of Browse with that many names opens, the link to the analyzer opens at every size of the table, and the help page and the book carry the limit.
- Exact Glob results keep their meaning, including the difference between the one-character `?` and the arbitrary-length `*`; the Contains, Glob and Regex modes return what they returned before.
- The measurement of the exact search and of the approximate candidates on 500,000 names is recorded with a recommendation (done).
- A Contains search that finds fewer than 10 exact matches shows up to 20 similar names below the list, found by uFuzzy for a typo or a missing character; a search with 10 or more exact matches, a search of fewer than three characters, and the Glob and Regex modes, a chosen prefix button, a chosen Show report and the answer of the server show none; a click on a similar name selects it; "Select matches" and the count of matches do not include it; the exact modes return what they returned before; on a list of 500,000 names the similar search adds less than 30 ms to a query and the memory of the page grows by less than 50 MB; the file of the library matches the SHA-256 that its record names, and a changed copy is rejected by the interface static checks.
- Revision: a text equal to the name of an exact match, with any letter case, shows no similar names, while a text that is a part of a name, a typo and a missing character return what they returned before the change; the help page and the book say that the list is not shown for a name typed in full.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- Decision Date: 2026-10-29. Record the viewer requests as later work; implementation and dependency selection remain pending.
- No execution dependency is established. Coordinate shared selection handling with M22, which is Complete: M22 left the selection and the Find modes unchanged and made the names of the address independent of the menu.
- Source inspection on 2026-10-29 and 2026-11-01 (read from the source tree): `browse.js` holds `LIMITS = { table: 100, chart: 5, concurrentRequests: 6, indexed: 500000 }`; a result list of at most 50,000 names reaches `state.results` with `state.total` the number of matches, and `state.partial` is on when the server answered because the names exceed the index limit; the selection changes only through `toggle(name)`, which adds one name up to `LIMITS.table` and otherwise writes "The table holds at most 100 PVs."; the list tools are "Selected only" and "Clear"; the address holds the selected names (`pv`) cut at `LIMITS.table`; the status of all the selected PVs is read at each refresh and the table is drawn for all of them; `names-worker.js` implements exact Contains, Glob and Regex matching. The book states the measurement behind the present limit: with 100 PVs at 1 Hz and ten minutes of samples each, every row of the table is filled in 0.65 s or less and the slowest data request takes under 50 ms; larger tables were not measured.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02. The owner directed that the findings of the first third-person review of this plan be applied: the address of the analyzer is limited by the server (350 names answered 200 and 400 names answered HTTP 400 on the appliance of 2026-11-02, with names of the form `SR:BPM:NNN:X`), so the link carries the first selected PVs that fit in 6 KB; "Select matches" is off while "Selected only" is on; the limit of the table is not fixed by the plan but set by the owner from the recorded numbers; and the measured version of uFuzzy is recorded (the latest on npm was 1.0.19 on 2026-11-02). The selected PVs are sent to the server for their status in a POST body, which has no such limit.
- Recommendation recorded on 2026-11-02 from T3 (the adoption of a search library or of an approximate-match control in the interface is not decided by this row): uFuzzy is the lightest in memory (+0.52 GB, the least of the candidates), answers in 5 to 8 ms with no preparation (first query 7 to 9 ms), is 8.4 KB in one file and is the only candidate that finds a typo; fuzzysort answers in 0 to 1 ms once its list is prepared and orders results best, but it needs 345 to 473 ms for the first query and about 0.45 GB more memory than uFuzzy; fzf and microfuzz find a missing character but not a typo, take 33 to 515 ms and use more memory than uFuzzy. A use in the interface would keep the exact results first and add a separate list of similar names, found by uFuzzy (typo, then missing characters) when the exact search finds none or few, and never used by "Select matches". A file placed in the repository needs its SHA-256 recorded and the interface static checks extended for it.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02. The owner directed to use uFuzzy in the interface ("just go with uFuzzy") and accepted the recommended form: a separate list of similar names under the exact matches, shown for a Contains search of at least three characters with fewer than 10 exact matches, at most 20 names, up to 10 names of the typo setting first and up to 10 of the setting for missing characters after them, never part of "Select matches"; the plan is revised for it and reviewed before the work starts. The library goes to `js/vendor/` with its record, because the page allows scripts of its own origin only and a worker can load a file of its own origin with `importScripts`.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02. The owner directed that the findings of the first review of this revision be applied: the list of similar names is shown only when no prefix button and no Show report is chosen and the Worker holds the names, because the exact search is filtered by them and a similar name from outside the filter would contradict it; the two passes of uFuzzy are joined typo setting first and cut at 20; and the memory of T7 is the mean of three runs, because the resident memory of the browser varies by 20 to 30 MB between runs.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02. The owner directed to apply the result of the first run of T6: for `SR:C1:BPM1:X` the typo setting alone found ten names (`SR:C10:BPM1:X` to `SR:C19:BPM1:X`), so the setting for missing characters did not run and `SR:C01:BPM1:X` was missing; both settings now run and give up to 10 names each, the typo setting first, cut at 20. The earlier text, in which the second setting ran only when the first found fewer than 10 names, is replaced.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02. The owner directed that the findings of the first reviews of the implementation be applied: `LICENCES/NOTICE` names uFuzzy 1.0.19 (MIT) among the browser assets and `LICENCES/ufuzzy_license.txt` carries its license text, in the way of the chart library, because that inventory lists every asset that the web applications ship and states that a change of a dependency requires updating it; and the help page and the book say that the list of similar names is not shown when more than 500,000 PVs are archived, because the page then does not hold the list of names and the server answers each search. Observations left as they are: a query of 300 equal characters takes 121 ms in the typo setting of uFuzzy (usual queries take 6 to 13 ms), and a name typed in full is still offered similar names when fewer than 10 PVs match it.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02. The owner set the limit of the table at 100 after the measurement of T2 (the table holds at most 100 PVs, 5 are plotted): 100 is already many, 300 would not be needed to find PVs to plot, and 1,000 stops the page. The limit stays 100, so the help page and the book keep their statement; the cut of the analyzer link to 6 KB is needed only if the limit is raised above about 350 names and is not built for 100 names (an address of 100 names is 2.4 KB and the viewer and `analyzer.html` answered 200 for it). The control "Select matches" adds at most as many PVs as the 100 leave room for. Whether a reader may raise the limit as a setting, with a note of its cost, is a question of the owner that this row does not decide.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02. The owner directed that the findings of the first second-person review of this plan be applied: a cut of the analyzer link is told in the visible note of the table as well as in the title of the link, and the measurement of the search names how the list of 500,000 names is built and how the memory is read.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02. The owner decided that the list of similar names is hidden when the name is typed in full, and that the limit of the table is not made a setting. The observation left as it was ("a name typed in full is still offered similar names when fewer than 10 PVs match it") is therefore replaced by the revision of the scope. The row is changed as its own follow-up work; the issue #73 stays closed.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The owner decided that the native suggestions of names (`<datalist>`) and the removal of `autocomplete="off"` are done in M22; this row keeps the selection of all matching PVs and the evaluation of ranked approximate matches against that native result.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The owner directed the shape of the work: if performance allows, more than 100 PVs can be selected, so that a user can find and keep them (100 is the number of PVs the table holds and 5 the number plotted); the limit is set by measurement; the search should improve by a lightweight and efficient method, which the plan measures before choosing; and the plan follows the recommended choices: the control adds as many matches as the limit leaves room for and says how many were left out, and an approximate result stays apart from the exact results.
- Candidates read on 2026-11-01. uFuzzy (MIT): about 7.5 KB, no index (start below 1 ms, near-zero memory), the author reports 5 ms for a search of three terms over 162,000 phrases, out-of-order terms, a single typo per term as an option, case-insensitive only, based on regular expressions and tuned for Latin text, with ranking meant for 1,000 results or fewer; fuzzysort (MIT): scores from 0 to 1 and the positions of the match, prepared targets (`snapshot()`, `prepare()`) for speed, the claim "under 1 ms for 13,000 files" without a method; Fuse.js is described as slower and larger; a trigram index needs memory for the index and a query of three characters. These figures are those of the authors, not measured here: the sources are the README files of https://github.com/leeoniya/uFuzzy and https://github.com/farzher/fuzzysort and web search results of 2026-11-01.

Check: T2. Done on 2026-11-02 in commit d6bb20f9a046d732a230a73bb84c92c7f7aeec3b. The limit stays 100 by the decision of 2026-11-02, so the cut of the analyzer link was not built.

#### M24 - Explain sampling changes and hide irrelevant confirmation controls

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M24
Identity History: none
GitHub Issue: #74 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/74)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

The operator reports an unlabeled checkbox in Change sampling and needs an explanation of its controls. The dialog shares `maintainAckRow` with operations such as type conversion, whose checkbox confirms a data backup; sampling supplies no confirmation text and should hide the row.

##### Scope

- Correct the visibility of the unexpected checkbox in the shipped dialog, which the reproduction of 2026-10-31 confirmed (see the decisions), without removing confirmations required by other operations.
- Explain MONITOR and SCAN, the meaning of their period, and how the MONITOR period affects sample-buffer sizing rather than imposing an event-throttling interval. The full explanation is on the help page `help.html`; the dialog carries a short text and a link to it.
- Make the explanations of the other console pages that advise on the sampling period agree with it: the two cards of the Operations page that advise a longer period for a fast PV, and the Archive page, which takes a method and a period without saying what the period means.
- State the behavior of a sampling change using the actual backend path, including its effect on existing data and any collection interruption that the implementation entails.

Out of scope: changing acquisition behavior, buffer sizing rules or sampling defaults; unrelated dialog redesign; a figure of the sample buffer, which goes into the mdBook after this row as M29.

##### Completion Criteria

- Change sampling has no unlabeled or irrelevant checkbox, including after another maintenance dialog was opened.
- Type conversion still displays and requires its backup confirmation.
- No console page advises a longer period as a way to slow a Monitor PV, and the Archive page says what the period of each method means.
- The dialog states method and period briefly and links to the help page, which opens in a new tab and explains accurately enough to distinguish MONITOR buffer sizing from SCAN timing, and the link and the page remain usable with keyboard navigation.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- Decision Date: 2026-10-29. Record the reported control and explanation problem for later implementation.
- Source inspection on 2026-10-29: `openMaintain` sets `maintainAckRow.hidden` from `config.ack`; `openChangeSampling` provides no `ack`, while `openChangeType` does. `browse.css` sets `dialog label` to `display: block`.
- Reproduction on 2026-10-31 11:25 PDT confirmed the cause: in the shipped dialog of Change sampling the row has `hidden` true and a computed display of `block`, drawn 398 by 22.5 px as an unlabeled checkbox, on the first opening and after the dialog Change the type had been opened and closed. Change the type shows its backup text and its checkbox is required. The row is therefore always visible in Change sampling; no earlier dialog is needed. Environment: the mgmt, engine, etl and retrieval WARs `aa-20261031-2c05b376-*`, whose `index.html`, `browse.js` and `browse.css` equal the sources, one appliance and a soft IOC with one record in a private network namespace, headless Firefox. Evidence: `work/m24-repro/browse-result.json`, `work/m24-repro/sampling-first.png` (SHA-256 11f4c4e84292c5f972699617fb0ec8e548e009c864684431a63b397e490fcb11), `work/m24-repro/type-conversion.png`, `work/m24-repro/sampling-after-type.png`, driver `work/m24-repro/run.sh` and `work/m24-repro/browse.py`. These files are in the untracked `work/m24-repro` of the workstation that holds this checkout. To repeat, run `unshare -rn bash work/m24-repro/run.sh` from the repository root: in a private network namespace it starts a soft IOC and the four WARs linked in `work/m24-repro/wars`, archives one record, drives the browser and writes `browse-result.json` and the three screenshots; the paths of the soft IOC and of Tomcat are fixed in `run.sh` for this workstation.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. The owner chose to correct the visibility in `browse.css` with one rule that restores `display: none` for an element with the `hidden` attribute inside a dialog, over a rule for the one label only and over adding or removing the row from the document. Source check on the same date: the only element with `hidden` inside a dialog of the console pages is `maintainAckRow` in `index.html`; `operations.html`, `external.html` and `archive.html` have none, and `external.css` has its own `dialog label` rule that this change does not touch.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. The owner chose to keep the text in the Change sampling dialog short and to put the full explanation on the help page `help.html`, which the dialog links to; the book FAQ is left as it is. Source check on the same date: `help.html` has no explanation of sampling or the buffer, and `faq.md` already states the capacity formula and the drop of the oldest sample, correct for the current formula, but it does not say that the period is the shortest time expected between two changes or how to choose the period.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. The owner directed that the open points of the plan review be settled as proposed: the present dialog text is replaced by the short text of step 3, and the link to the help page opens in a new tab so that the dialog stays open.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. The owner decided that `help.html` receives the explanation as text only, that this row closes with that text, and that the figure of the sample buffer is added to the mdBook afterwards as separate work.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. After the review of the implementation the owner directed that its findings be applied to the accepted plan: a PV is exempt from the rebuild only when it stays Scan and its period changes by less than 0.1 s, the dialog text is in two paragraphs, and the link text says that the page opens in a new tab. This revision of the plan is accepted by that direction, and the implementation authority of 2026-10-31 covers it.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. The owner directed that the help also say what happens to a PV that updates faster than the minimum period allows: its buffer cannot be sized for it and it can lose samples. The explanation of how to choose the period in step 3 includes it.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. After the review of the implementation the owner directed that the explanations of the other console pages be brought in line in this row. Source check on the same date of every text of the console that mentions the sampling period or Change sampling: `operations.js` advises "Slow the fastest PVs with Change sampling" in the card of the write and "a longer period with Change sampling" in the card of the fastest PVs, which does not slow a Monitor PV because its period only sizes the buffer; `archive.html` takes a method and a period with no statement of their meaning; the texts of `system.js`, `judge.js`, `operations.js` (buffer overflow) and `browse.js` that advise a shorter period for a buffer overflow agree with the explanation and stay.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. The owner directed that the wording "the fastest rate you expect" for the period be replaced, in the dialog, the help and the hint of the Archive page, by "the shortest time you expect between two changes", because the field takes seconds and a reader could type a rate.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. The owner directed that two observations of the third review of the implementation be applied: the cards of the Operations page say that a PV on Scan already needs only a longer period, and the label of the Scan option in the dialog says that it stores at most once per period instead of at a fixed period.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. The owner directed that the findings of the second-person review of the console texts be applied: the help says where the real interval of a PV is taken from and why the Details rate is not that, and the card of the write names Browse PVs. Source and run check on the same date: for a PV that posts twice per second against a period of 1.0 s the Details row "Estimated event rate (events/sec)" read 1.14, because `ArchiveChannel` counts an event only when `SampleBuffer.add` accepted it without a drop, so the row tends to the capacity divided by the write period.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. The owner noted that the sizing of the buffer is expected to change substantially later. The dialog text, the section of the help, the hint of the Archive page and the two cards of the Operations page describe the buffer as it is built now (a capacity fixed when the channel is created and sized from the period) and are revised together with any such change. The ideas and the findings for that change are recorded in M15, whose status is Deferred.
- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. The owner directed that the finding of the third second-person review be applied (the table of the fastest PVs on the Operations page gives only a name and a rate, so the card names the way to the dialog), and that the reviews and the checks that remained be completed before this row counts as done.
- Observed on 2026-10-31: issue #74 still carries the first draft of 2026-10-31 00:12 PDT. Its Current Behavior calls the cause suspected and not reproduced, its proposal explains the controls in the dialog alone, its third acceptance criterion names no help page or link, and its out of scope lacks the mdBook figure (M29). On 2026-10-31 22:14 PDT the body was replaced with the projection of this detail, a closure comment named the verified result and the issue was closed as completed, under the issue delegation of the owner.
- Source inspection on 2026-10-31 of the sample buffer, which the help states. A MONITOR channel stores every change it receives; its period never limits the rate and only sizes the buffer: the capacity is the write period divided by the period, rounded to a whole number and at least 1, plus the margin in seconds divided by the period rounded up (at least one), and the total is at least 2. With the defaults (write period `secondsToBuffer` 10 s, `sampleBufferMarginSeconds` 1.0 s) a period of 1.0 s gives 11 slots and a period of 0.1 s gives 110. One executed check of `ArchiveEngine.computeBufferCapacity` returned 11, 110, 3 and 22 for periods 1.0, 0.1, 5.0 and 0.5. The capacity is fixed when the channel is created. All buffers are written at the same instant every write period. When more samples arrive between two writes than the capacity holds, each further sample removes the oldest one and counts as a buffer-full loss, so the earliest samples of the interval are the ones missing. One executed run of the real `SampleBuffer` (program `work/m24-repro/Ovf.java`, untracked, compiled against `target/classes`, log4j-api, protobuf-java, commons-lang3 and json-simple) added 20 samples to a buffer of 11 slots, kept the newest 11 (values 10 to 20) and counted 9 buffer-full losses. The other mechanisms are read from the source, not executed.
- Source inspection on 2026-10-31 of the sampling change: the management action first stores the new method and period in the type information, and sends the change to the engine only when the PV is not paused, so a paused PV takes the change when it resumes. The engine stops the channel, removes it and its write-buffer entry and archives the PV again, which also applies when a MONITOR PV stays MONITOR; a PV that stays SCAN and whose period differs by less than 0.1 s is not rebuilt, so its running channel keeps the old period while the stored type information holds the new one; a change of the method, from SCAN to MONITOR as well, or a larger change of the period rebuilds it. A rebuilt channel interrupts the collection for the time of the rebuild; the stored data does not change.
- No execution dependency is established.

The stored data does not change."), names the method and the period, keeps the statement that the stored data does not change, adds that a paused PV takes the change when it resumes and that a running PV is rebuilt with a short interruption, except a PV that stays SCAN when its period changes by less than 0.1 s, and links to the help page in a new tab so that the dialog stays open, the link text saying that it opens in a new tab, and the label of the Scan option in the dialog saying that it stores at most once per period; and the full explanation on `help.html` with the two methods, the period of each, that the MONITOR period is the shortest time expected between two changes and sizes the buffer without limiting what is stored, which samples are lost when it is exceeded, how to choose the period (not larger than the real interval between updates of the PV, so that the buffer holds one write interval of them, and not below the minimum the dialog field enforces, a site setting of the appliance; a PV that updates faster than that minimum allows cannot have a buffer sized for it and can lose samples; the real interval is taken from what the operator knows of the PV, because the Estimated event rate of Details counts only the samples the buffer accepted and reads lower than the real rate for a PV that loses samples), the rebuild of the channel with its short interruption, the unchanged stored data, the paused PV that takes the change at resume, and the SCAN period change below 0.1 s of a PV that stays SCAN, which updates the stored setting but leaves the running channel unchanged, while a change of the method or a larger change of the period rebuilds it. Check: T2.
4. Bring the other pages in line: in `js/operations.js` the cards of the write and of the fastest PVs advise changing a fast Monitor PV to Scan with a longer period, a PV that is on Scan already needing only a longer period, the card of the write naming Browse PVs as the place of Change sampling, the card of the fastest PVs naming the way to the dialog (find the PV by name in Browse PVs, open More, choose Change sampling), and say that a longer period on a Monitor PV only makes its buffer smaller; in `archive.html` the hint of the sampling choice says that Monitor stores every change and uses the period only to size its buffer and that Scan stores the latest value at most once per period; in `help.html` the item of the Archive page about the sampling choice says the same in a sentence and links to the section of step 3. Check: T3.

#### M29 - Show the sample buffer path, slots and overflow in the mdBook

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M29
Identity History: none
GitHub Issue: #75 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/75)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

The explanation of the sample buffer that M24 puts on the help page as text is easier to follow with a figure, and the owner decided on 2026-10-31 that the figure belongs in the mdBook, after M24. The book states the capacity formula and the drop of the oldest sample in `faq.md` and describes the engine buffer in `architecture.md`, but shows no figure of the path of an event through the buffer.

##### Scope

- Three figures in the mdBook, drawn as SVG files under `docs/book/src/images` with their own light background so that they are readable on every theme of the book, each with a text alternative: (1) the path of an event of a MONITOR PV through the checks of the engine (`wrong timestamp`), the buffer (`type change`, `buffer full`), the swap of the buffer at each write period, which happens only when the buffer holds samples, and the write to the short term store, which retries the same batch while new samples fill the new buffer when a write fails, with the three places where a sample is lost marked; (2) the capacity of the buffer for a given period, with the examples of 1.0 s with 11 slots and of 0.1 s with 110; (3) the samples kept and dropped when more samples arrive between two writes than the buffer holds (20 samples into 11 slots keep the newest 11 and lose the oldest 9), and the update rate above which the loss starts (the capacity divided by the write period).
- The figures and their text are one new section "The sample buffer" of `architecture.md`, placed after the engine bullets of its data-flow description; `faq.md` keeps its text and gets one sentence that links to the section.
- One unit test in the repository that pins the overflow numbers of figure 3 on the real `SampleBuffer` (20 samples into 11 slots: the newest 11 kept, 9 counted as buffer-full losses), next to `SampleBufferOverFlowTest`, because `SampleBufferCapacityTest` already pins 11 and 110 but no test pins the kept and dropped samples.
- The figures and their text agree with the current code and with the help page of M24.

Out of scope: changing acquisition behavior, buffer sizing rules or sampling defaults; the help page and the dialog of M24; a figure on any page of the console; a figure of the case in which a PV updates more slowly than its period and leaves slots empty (the help page of M24 says it in text); the history of the earlier capacity formula.

##### Completion Criteria

- The three figures are SVG files in the book source that the book references, each with a text alternative, and they are readable on the light and the coal theme of the book.
- The section "The sample buffer" of `architecture.md` carries them, and `faq.md` links to it.
- Every number in the figures and their text equals the value returned by the current code for the same inputs, and the overflow numbers are pinned by a unit test that passes.
- The book builds with the `aa-mdbook` image, which holds the mdBook and admonish versions that the CI uses, and every link and fragment of the generated pages resolves.
- The title and the body of issue #75 describe the three figures, the new section and the test before the issue is closed.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. The owner decided to add the figure to the mdBook after M24 closes, as separate work, and to put it in the Milestone section. On the same date the owner added this row to release Ahsoka, after M24 and before the release (D15). The row depends on M24 because the figure must agree with the explanation M24 writes.
- Source of the figure: a draft page made on 2026-10-31 outside the repository. Its values were checked against the current code on the same date: `ArchiveEngine.computeBufferCapacity` returned 11 for a period of 1.0 s and 110 for 0.1 s with a write period of 10 s and a margin of 1 s, and a run of the real `SampleBuffer` (program `work/m24-repro/Ovf.java`, untracked) with 11 slots and 20 samples kept the newest 11 and counted 9 buffer-full losses.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The owner directed that the findings of the first second-person review of this plan be applied: the title and the body of issue #75 ("Show the sample buffer path and sizing in a figure of the manual") describe one figure, so before the issue is closed they are replaced with the projection of this detail through the issue operation, as was done for issues #72 and #74; the link check of T1 is a script that is named in the test, not a check described in words.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. #### M30 - Review the sampling policies against measured event rates and decide the optimized values

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M30
Identity History: none
GitHub Issue: #76 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/76)
Status: Not started

##### Summary

The shipped `policies.py` gives every policy, including `2HzPVs`, which applies above 2 events per second, a MONITOR period of 1.0 s, so every such PV gets a buffer of 11 slots and a PV above about 1.1 events per second loses samples in every write interval (findings of M15 of 2026-10-31). The `BPMS` policy raises the period to limit the stored rate, which does not limit what a MONITOR PV stores and only shrinks its buffer. The owner decided on 2026-10-31 that release Ahsoka reviews the policies against the measured sampling rates and periods and decides optimized values (D13).

##### Scope

- Inventory what each policy of `src/sitespecific/default/classpathfiles/policies.py` and of its copy for the tests decides (method, period, stores) and which inputs the policy receives at the archive request (the event rate and the storage rate measured then).
- Measure on the real path, for PVs at several event rates and for the periods the shipped policies assign, the buffer capacity, the buffer-full losses and the completeness of the stored samples over bounded windows; include the effect of the minimum sampling period, which bounds the rate a period can size a buffer for.
- Decide with the owner the policy values: how the MONITOR period follows the event rate measured at the archive request so that the buffer holds a write interval and its margin; the rule for a PV faster than the minimum period allows; the rule for a PV whose rate is not yet known; the `BPMS` policy; and whether the settings `secondsToBuffer`, `sampleBufferMarginSeconds` and `minimumSamplingPeriod` change.
- Change the shipped policies and their copy for the tests accordingly, and state the policies in the book.

Out of scope: the automatic adjustment of a buffer at run time and the study of the long-term reliability of the stored signal (M15); changes to the engine code; the policies of any other site.

##### Completion Criteria

- A decision records the measured table and the chosen policy values with its date.
- The shipped policies implement the decision, and a PV archived under each policy at its expected event rate loses no sample to a full buffer over a bounded window on the real path, while the loss of a PV above that rate is still counted.
- The tests of the policies pass and the book states the policies.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- D13 (Decision Date 2026-10-31): the review of the sampling policies is a row of release Ahsoka.
- The findings that this row answers are recorded in M15 (findings of 2026-10-31): the default period of 1.0 s for every policy, the counter that understates the input of a PV that loses samples, and the effect of the period of a MONITOR PV on its buffer only.
- The explanation of the sampling period written by M24 describes the buffer as built today; a change of the policy values does not change that explanation, a change of the sizing of the buffer does.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: draft
Plan Acceptance: none
Implementation Authorization: none
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

1. Inventory the policies and measure their effect on the real path (the soft IOC with PVs at several rates, one appliance). Check: T1.
2. Decide the policy values with the owner from the measured table.
3. Change the policies and the book, and run the tests of the policies. Checks: T2, T3.

##### Test Plan

| Label | Layer | Method | Environment | Expected Result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T1 | Real path measurement | Archive PVs that post at several event rates under the shipped policies; read the capacity and the buffer-full losses from `getPVDetails` and retrieve the stored samples over a bounded window | Packaged WARs, a soft IOC, one appliance | A table of event rate, policy, period, capacity, losses and stored completeness |
| T2 | Policy tests | The tests of `policies.py` and of the policy selection of the archive request | Local build | The tests pass with the decided values |
| T3 | Real path regression | Archive PVs at their expected event rates under the changed policies and over-rate PVs | Packaged WARs, a soft IOC, one appliance | No buffer-full loss at the expected rates; the loss above them is counted |

##### Verification Results

| Label | Observed At | Environment | Result | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| T2 | Not run | Local build | Pending | none |
| T3 | Not run | Packaged WARs, a soft IOC, one appliance | Pending | none |

##### Summary

The verification of the Find work needed four things that other work will need again: building the book with the `aa-mdbook` image and checking its links, generating a set of test PVs, and starting a development appliance with the static content of the working tree in a private network namespace. They are written first under `scripts/` of this repository, in dash and in Perl (commit 2e36f4aaa457e99e51928530dbb2037e943ca9c4), and belong in the owner's script tool, where they are kept and reused from one place.

Move the scripts written under `scripts/` for the book build and its link check, the test PV set and the development appliance to the owner's script tool, keep them working against this repository from there, and settle which copy governs.

Out of scope: the Python drivers that steer the browser; a change of the continuous integration workflows unless the plan decides it; scripts that are not listed here.

#### M27 - Tell chart lines apart without color alone

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M27
Identity History: none
GitHub Issue: #71 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/71)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

Plots need distinguishable line styles as well as colors. Operators need display options for differing color-vision needs, with their selected time and display settings reused during the browser session.

##### Scope

- Three display profiles for the charts, chosen by the reader like the time zone: Default; Red-green (for a reader who cannot tell red from green, whether weakly or not at all); Monochrome (for a reader who sees no color). The choice is one for the whole application, offered in a selection box at the right end of the menu bar next to the time zone, kept in the browser store so that it survives closing and reopening the browser, and read from the address (a parameter that the address wins over the store with, as `tz` does) so that it can be carried to the data viewer. The menu entry of the Analyzer and the links that open the analyzer from Browse carry the parameter in their address, which `analyzer-forward.js` passes on to the viewer; today only the links of Browse carry `tz`, and the menu entry carries nothing.
- In every profile each plotted line gets its own combination of color, dash pattern and marker, and no two lines of a chart share a combination. The color and the dash pattern alone keep every line of the supported number apart (in the Monochrome profile the dash pattern and the lightness), because the marker is drawn only where the chart marks its samples, which it does not on a dense line; the marker is an added cue. The number of combinations of a profile is the number of lines the profile supports, which is at least five in every profile now (the chart limit of Browse); the legend shows the same combination as the line. Further lines (the analyzer will plot more than the five lines of today) are supported by adding combinations to the table of the profile, not by changing the code that draws.
- The profile applies to every chart of the application that has a legend (Browse, Retrieval, Operations) and to the data viewer, which is served by the retrieval web application on its own port and draws its lines today with one CSS color and a dash pattern by position, so that its first two lines look alike; the viewer draws its lines through the combination of the profile after this row. The analyzer of the later rows keeps the same profile and the same table by drawing its lines through the same shared code; no analyzer work is done in this row.
- The colors of each profile give every line a contrast of at least 3:1 against the chart background, and the lines stay distinguishable when the browser forces its own colors, because the dash pattern remains.
- Say the new behavior where the operator reads about it: one sentence in the help page and one in the page of the book that describes the time zone, that the menu bar has a profile next to the time zone, that it is kept in the browser and passed to the data viewer.

Out of scope: changing archived samples, acquisition settings, account-wide preferences or cross-device synchronization; the color-vision profiles that are not listed (a profile for the blue-yellow deficiency, which is very rare, is covered by the Monochrome profile); new chart types; the analyzer pages themselves.

##### Completion Criteria

- The three profiles exist, are chosen from the menu bar, and the choice survives closing and reopening the browser, as the time zone does.
- With each profile, five PVs plotted together have five different combinations of color, dash and marker, and the legend shows the combination of its line; a profile has no repeated combination below its supported number of lines, which is at least five, and every pair of its lines differs by color and dash (by dash and lightness in Monochrome) without the marker.
- The data viewer uses the profile that the console carries to it in the address, from the Analyzer entry of the menu as well as from the links of Browse, keeps it in its own store, and draws its lines with the same combinations as the console.
- The lines of each profile have a contrast of at least 3:1 against the chart background, and a chart rendered through the simulations of protanopia, deuteranopia, tritanopia and achromatopsia keeps every pair of lines apart by dash or lightness without the marker.
- The profile code is shared, so that the analyzer can use the same table and the same selection without a second implementation.
- The help page and the book say how the profile is chosen, kept and carried.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- Decision Date: 2026-10-30. Record this viewer follow-up after the current PVA metadata implementation. This is a planning request, not implementation authorization.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The owner directed the shape of the work: reader profiles chosen like the time zone and changing the style of the charts, a choice that survives reopening the browser, the data viewer included, and the later analyzer keeping the same style. The profiles are three: the general reader, the reader who cannot tell colors apart (red and green), and the reader who sees no color; the owner described the weak and the absent red-green sense as one profile of readers who "cannot tell colors apart", and the plan keeps them together because the same colors serve both.
- Survey of 2026-11-01 (read from the source tree). The chart is the shared module `staticcontent/js/aa/chart`: `series.js` holds the palette `PALETTE` of ten colors and the dash list `DASHES` that is `["", "", "6 3", "2 3", "9 3 2 3"]`, so the first two lines are both solid and differ only by two close colors (`#007681`, `#5a6b70`); `lineLook(series, slot)` gives color, dash and width to `draw.js` and `legend.js`, which therefore agree. The charts of Browse, Retrieval and Operations and the data viewer use this module; the chart limit of Browse is five lines. The alarm mark of a sample is a small rectangle in one color (`--alarm`). The chart options (line shape, Y scale, marks, average, band) are kept only in the memory of the page. The time zone is kept by `aa/timezone.js` in `localStorage` under `aa.timeZone`, offered by a selection box at the right end of the menu bar, read from `tz` of the address or the query, and carried to the analyzer in the address by the links of Browse that open the analyzer (the menu entry of the Analyzer carries nothing); the data viewer loads neither that module nor a menu bar.
- Standards read on 2026-11-01: WCAG 2.2 Success Criterion 1.4.1 Use of Color (Level A) requires that color is not the only visual means of conveying information or distinguishing a visual element, and technique G111 (Using color and pattern) is a sufficient technique for it; Success Criterion 1.4.11 Non-text Contrast (Level AA) requires a contrast of at least 3:1 against adjacent colors for the parts of a graphic that are needed to understand it, with lines of a line graph named as an example; the CSS media feature `forced-colors` (Baseline since 2022-09) replaces author colors, including SVG `stroke`, with system colors. The sources are the W3C Understanding pages of 1.4.1 and 1.4.11, the technique G111 and the MDN page of `forced-colors`. No web standard defines profiles for color-vision deficiency or a number of line styles; the profiles of this row are the application's own. The color-vision types are the normal trichromacy, the anomalous trichromacy (protanomaly, deuteranomaly, tritanomaly), the dichromacy (protanopia, deuteranopia, tritanopia) and the monochromacy (achromatopsia); Chrome's tool for emulating vision deficiencies offers protanopia, deuteranopia, tritanopia and achromatopsia, and its notes say that design for these extremes also serves the milder forms. The Okabe-Ito palette (orange, sky blue, bluish green, yellow, blue, vermillion, reddish purple, black), designed for readers of every color vision, is a candidate for the Red-green profile; its values were taken from secondary sources and the original guide gives them only as a figure. Not verified: the prevalence figures (taken from one educational site) and `prefers-contrast`, whose page was not read.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The owner directed that the findings of the first third-person review of this plan be applied: the profile is carried to the viewer by the Analyzer entry of the menu and by the links of Browse, because only the links of Browse (`browse.js`, line 769) add `tz` to the address today; the viewer draws its lines through the combination of the profile; the color and the dash alone keep the supported lines apart and the marker is an added cue, because the chart draws markers only where it shows its samples (`MARKER_LIMIT` of `draw.js`); and the test of forced colors names its two settings. Facts checked by that review: the colors of the present palette have a contrast of at least 4.18:1 against white, the first two lines (`#007681`, `#5a6b70`) have 1.04:1 against each other and are both solid; in Firefox 153 `browser.display.document_color_use` 2 with `ui.useAccessibilityTheme` 1 make `forced-colors` active, and an SVG stroke attribute stayed unchanged in that probe, so the result under forced colors is measured, not assumed.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The owner directed that the findings of the first second-person review of this plan be applied: the text on forced colors names the dash pattern, not the marker, as the cue that remains; every profile supports at least five lines now; and the survey says that only the links of Browse carry the time zone to the analyzer.
- Implementation choices of 2026-11-01 within the accepted plan: the Red-green colors were chosen from a grid of colors with at least 3.2:1 against white and against the chart background by the largest least CIEDE2000 difference under normal vision, protanopia, deuteranopia and tritanopia (the Okabe-Ito palette fails 3:1 for four of its colors on this background, so it was not used as it is); Monochrome is five steps of lightness down to the lightest gray that keeps 3.2:1; Default keeps the colors of the present palette and gives its first five lines five different dash patterns. Each profile has eight or ten combinations, the first five differ from each other in color and in dash pattern, and the table is the one place to add combinations. A chart without a legend draws its lines through the combinations when the page passes `combinations: true`, which the data viewer does; its labels at the end of the lines keep their own color.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The owner directed that the findings of the first third-person review of the implementation and of the first second-person review of its documents be applied: the menu script no longer follows the `storage` event, because with two tabs the Analyzer entry of the first tab carried the profile chosen in the second while the first tab still showed its own, and the time zone has no such behavior; and the book names the form of the session-store key. Observations left as they are: the histogram bars and the statistics rows of the Y projection are told apart by the color of their line only, which the plan does not cover (lines only); the labels at the end of the lines of the viewer keep their own color; a wrong `profile` value in an address shows Default for that page while the store keeps the earlier choice, as `tz` does; the menu bar was seen at the narrowest width the browser allowed (about 500 px) and not at a phone width.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-01. The owner decided that the "time settings" of the original request are the time zone, which `aa/timezone.js` already keeps in the browser; the time range of a chart stays in the address of the page, and this row adds no other time setting.
- Coordinate preference storage with the restoration of M22, which is Complete: it keeps the last address of a page in `sessionStorage`; the profile is a separate setting in `localStorage`, so the two do not interact.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: accepted
Plan Acceptance: 2026-11-01; owner acceptance of the plan after two third-person and two second-person passes, the last of each with no finding above the floor
Implementation Authorization: 2026-11-01, owner directed to implement and verify the accepted plan
Superseded Plan Artifacts: the plan of 2026-10-30, which left the profiles and the session behavior to plan review

#### M28 - Study dynamic configuration reload and immediate API application

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M28
Identity History: none
GitHub Issue: #68 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/68)
Status: Not started

##### Summary

Selected configuration changes might be applied to a running appliance through a 60-second reload cycle or an explicit API request, while other settings must keep requiring a restart. This study decides which. `DefaultConfigService.initialize()` loads the installation properties at start. `EngineContext` sets `jca.use_env` to true before it creates the command threads, and each `JCACommandThread` constructs its own `CAJContext`. The execution-policy cache expires one minute after it is written and is read on demand; that is separate from a general configuration reload.

##### Scope

- Inventory the configuration settings by source, consumer and read time, and classify each as dynamically applicable, requiring recreation of the Channel Access context, or requiring a component or JVM restart, with code references.
- Compare 60-second polling with explicit API application: change detection, validation, concurrent updates, and how a caller distinguishes an accepted change from a successfully applied one.
- Evaluate Channel Access reconnection, channel and monitor recreation, pending commands, buffered samples and collection gaps, and define how interruption, loss and duplication would be measured.
- Define persistence across restarts and the precedence among environment variables, JVM properties, configuration files and API updates, separating changes to startup files from configuration available to a running process.
- Define behavior for invalid input, partial application, an unavailable component and a failed recreation of the Channel Access context, including retention or restoration of the last valid configuration.
- Define consistency across the management and engine components, access control of the API, and visibility of requested versus effective configuration.
- Produce a recommendation, the explicit unsupported cases and a verification plan on the real configuration and acquisition paths, and name the runtime behavior that remains unverified.

Out of scope: implementing configuration reload or an API; preparing environment-side `config.d` support; treating the existing policy-cache expiration as a general configuration reload.

##### Completion Criteria

- Every configuration setting has a recorded source, consumer, read time and classification, each supported by a code reference.
- The comparison of polling and explicit API application states change detection, validation, concurrent-update handling and the distinction between accepted and applied changes.
- Interruption, loss and duplication across the recreation of the Channel Access context are defined and measured on a real appliance with an IOC, or the reason a case cannot be measured is recorded.
- Persistence, precedence, failure behavior, cross-component consistency, access control and the visibility of requested versus effective configuration are defined.
- A recommendation, its unsupported cases and a verification plan are recorded, and the runtime behavior that remains unverified is named.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- Decision Date: 2026-10-31. Register issue #68 as backlog work. This entry records a study; no implementation of reload or of an API is authorized.
- No execution dependency is established.
- Decision Date: 2026-11-02 (D16). The owner moved this row from release Ahsoka to release Ghost; D15 no longer names it.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: draft
Plan Acceptance: none
Implementation Authorization: none
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

1. Inventory the settings through the shipped initialization and runtime paths (`DefaultConfigService.initialize()`, `EngineContext`, `JCACommandThread`), then classify each and record its code reference. Check: T1.
2. Compare polling and explicit API application and define persistence, precedence, validation, failure behavior, consistency, access control and visibility of requested versus effective configuration. Check: T1.
3. Measure the recreation of the Channel Access context under real acquisition: interruption, loss and duplication, and the effect on pending commands and buffered samples. Check: T2.
4. Write the recommendation with its unsupported cases, the verification plan and the unverified behavior. Checks: T1, T2.

##### Test Plan

| Label | Layer | Method | Environment | Expected Result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T1 | Source and runtime analysis | Trace each setting from its source to its consumer and read time in the shipped paths, then change a value on a running appliance and observe when the consumer reads it | Source tree and a local appliance | Each setting has a source, consumer, read time and classification confirmed by a code reference and an observation |
| T2 | Integration | Recreate the Channel Access context while a real IOC publishes known samples, then compare the samples archived and retrieved against the published ones | Local appliance, IOC and JDK 21 | Interruption, loss and duplication are measured and stated, and unmeasured cases are listed |

| T2 | Not run | Local appliance, IOC and JDK 21 | Pending | none |

##### Summary

Release Ahsoka carries twenty rows on the branch ahsoka: M12, M14, M7 and M13 (D12), M30 (D13), M33 (D17), M34 (D18) and M24, M29, M22, M27, M23, M10, M8, M11 and M1 to M5 (D15), worked in the order of the work table with M30 last by the owner's decision of 2026-10-31; the rows M25 and M26, which are Complete on the branch, are part of it. It follows release 2026-10, whose install and run check D11 accepts. This row owns the release-wide re-runs, the version change, the release actions and the final checks; the work of those rows is implemented, verified and accepted in their own details. The combined branch is the branch ahsoka once those rows are Complete. Phase 4 is the cycle planning of the `release-cycle` procedure, in which the owner reviews and accepts each plan and records the implementation authority separately. The soft IOC is the one of D3.

##### Scope

- Each of the twenty rows named in the Summary is Complete in its own detail before this row starts its checks.

- The project version in `pom.xml` is set to Ahsoka, the branch ahsoka is merged into master with a merge commit, the annotated tag Ahsoka is created on that merge commit, and every issue of those rows that is still open then closes through a `Closes` trailer of the version commit when it reaches master; on 2026-10-31 that is #45, #74, #72, #71, #73, #59, #62, #60, #75, #76 and #39, while #52, #47 and #41 are already closed; #39 is shared by M1 to M5 and closes only when all five are Complete.

- The documented install-from-bundle path runs on Debian 13 and on Rocky Linux 8.10 with the bundle built from the tag Ahsoka.
- A 24-hour run of the form of D11 runs on a bundle built from the version commit, before the merge into master (D16).

Out of scope: any other Backlog row, in particular M6, M9, M17 and M18, which D15 does not name, M28 and M16, which D16 moved to release Ghost, M15, which D13 leaves to the time after the release and D16 puts in release Ghost, and M19, M20 and G1, which D14 leaves out of the release; a run of the tag in the environment repository that is longer than the 24-hour run of Release Verification 15, which the owner decides after the release; the analyzer rows.

##### Release Execution

The owner's push trigger for steps 1, 3 and 5 covers the outgoing commits named in the step: the commits of those rows and the register commits, and at step 5 the merge commit as well; the executor reads them with the preflight of the Push scope. A push to a ref cancels the Maven run still in progress on that ref, so the executor waits for the run of the previous push to finish before the next push. The evidence of a step is the command output named in its Expected Result, copied into the Evidence column when the step runs.
