#### M33 - List the PVs that the engine paused for a type change

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M33
Identity History: none
GitHub Issue: #77 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/77)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

The report `getPVsByDroppedEventsTypeChange`, which the Browse PVs selection **Most events dropped by a type change** shows, skips every paused PV (`DroppedEventsTypeChangeReport`, `typeInfo.isPaused()`). Since the engine pauses a PV at its first event of another type (commit 9c940e5f, issue #46), such a PV is always paused and the report never lists it; the type information of the PV keeps the reason in `pauseReason`, which `getPVDetails` and the Details window show. Observed on 2026-11-03 in T2 of M13: the engine paused four PVs whose records changed their type, and the report was empty in three runs, also for a PV that was resumed without a type change and paused again. The section on type changes of `operating.md` says that the selection lists these PVs. Two facts deepen the cause: the engine removes the channel of the PV when it pauses it (`ArchiveEngine.pauseArchivingPV` ends in `destoryPv`), and the report reads only the channel list of the engine, so removing the paused check alone would not list the PV; and `eventsDropped` is the count `invalidTypeLostEventCount` of the channel's `PVMetrics`, kept in memory only, which goes with the channel. A PVAccess PV whose record came back with another type reaches the same pause through the sample buffer (`EPICS_V4_PV` re-reads the type from the structure after a reconnect, the buffer drops the first event of the other type, and `ArchiveChannel.addValueToBuffer` pauses the PV); this path was read in the code and never run. The readers of the report are the Browse PVs selection and its State column (`N lost`), the scripts `listTypeChanges.bash` and `checkTypeChangedPVs.bash` of the book, which a scheduler runs as an alert, and the live runner `verify_bash_samples.py`, which waits for the PV in the report.

##### Scope

- The report `getPVsByDroppedEventsTypeChange` of the management web application lists, in addition to the PVs that the engines report, every PV of the cluster (`getAllPVs`) whose type information is paused with a `pauseReason` that names a type change, under the same `limit` as the rows of the engines; such a row carries `pvName`, the old and the new type taken from the reason, and no `eventsDropped`, because that count went with the channel. The Browse PVs selection, the State column, the two scripts of the book and the live runner keep reading this one report.
- `listTypeChanges.bash` prints the old and the new type of such a PV from the report row, because the details of a paused PV carry no engine fields.
- The book and the note of the selection say what the report lists.
- The live runner `verify_bash_samples.py` compares each Bash script with the Python original it replaced, read from commit d1cd363c. For `checkTypeChangedPVs` the original and the Bash script still agree, because both read only `pvName`; for `listTypeChanges` the paused PV is an intentional difference that its judge names, because the original reads the details of such a PV, which carry no engine fields.

Out of scope: the conversion of the stored data of such a PV (M13); a PVAccess structure that changes while the connection stays up, which the engine neither counts nor pauses (an error line per event); the count of the events dropped before the pause, which is not kept.

##### Completion Criteria

- The report lists every PV that the engine paused for a type change, with its old and its new type, over Channel Access and over PVAccess, and the Browse PVs selection shows it; the alert script `checkTypeChangedPVs.bash` exits 1 for it and `listTypeChanges.bash` prints its two types.
- A test with a real engine channel whose record changes its type checks the report for a Channel Access PV and for a PVAccess PV, and fails on the code of today.
- The default suite, the tests of the two scripts and the live runner pass.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- D17 (Decision Date 2026-11-03): the row belongs to release Ahsoka; its place in the order is not set and it stands after M13.
- Decided at the plan review (Decision Date 2026-11-03): the report itself lists the paused PVs (the one place every reader uses), its rows for them carry the two types and no `eventsDropped`, and the test covers Channel Access and PVAccess.
- Observed at the implementation (2026-11-03): the report that the management returns is the rows of the engines followed by one row for each PV the engine paused for a type change; a row without `eventsDropped` is read as no count by the Browse PVs page and by the sums of the State column; five runs of T3 were needed because the check itself had four defects, none in the product.
- Below the floor, recorded for a later stage (reviews of 2026-11-03, owner confirmed): the check `work/run-m33-t3.bash` needed five runs because of its own defects; the item on type changes in `operating.md` now holds the convertible types, the element count, the window message and the check steps in one list item, which a later edit of that section may split.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: accepted
Plan Acceptance: 2026-11-03, after the plan review with three owner decisions (the report itself lists the paused PVs, the rows carry the two types and no count, the test covers Channel Access and PVAccess) and three third-person passes, the last two with no finding above the floor
Implementation Authorization: 2026-11-03, owner authorized implementing and verifying the accepted plan (steps 1 to 5); no commit or push is authorized by it
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

1. T1 first: a test in the manner of `TypeChangePauseTest` (a soft IOC, the PV archived as a double, the IOC replaced by one that serves the name as an integer), once with a Channel Access name and once with a `pva://` name, reads the report through the shipped `DroppedEventsTypeChangeReport` with a real `ConfigService` and fails on the code of today. Check: T1. The report also calls the engine URL of the test configuration over HTTP; no engine web application answers there, so that part of the report is empty in T1 and may wait for a connection timeout, and T3 checks the combined report.

In `src/main/org/epics/archiverappliance/mgmt/bpl/reports/DroppedEventsTypeChangeReport.java` the management report adds, after the rows of the engines, one row for every PV of the cluster (`getAllPVs`) whose type information is paused with a reason that names a type change, with `pvName`, `oldType` and `newType` parsed from the reason, under the same `limit`; the engine report is not changed. Check: T1.
3. `docs/book/src/samples/listTypeChanges.bash` takes the two types from the report row when the row has them and reads the details only otherwise; its test under `src/test/pythontests` covers a row of each kind. Check: T2.

5. In `src/test/pythontests/verify_bash_samples.py` the judge of `listTypeChanges` names the paused PV as an intentional difference from the Python original; the judge of `checkTypeChangedPVs` is not changed. Check: T3.

#### M34 - Make the retrieval metrics map safe for concurrent requests

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M34
Identity History: none
GitHub Issue: #78 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/78)
Status: Complete

##### Summary

`RetrievalState.updateRetrievalMetrics` records the time and the caller of each retrieval in `retrievalMetricsMap`, a plain `HashMap`, with `putIfAbsent` followed by `get`, from the request threads of the servlet. Two requests that arrive together, as the Analyzer sends one per PV and several per PV for the summary, the display and the min-max band, can race in that map, and `get` then returns null: the request fails with HTTP 500 (`DataRetrievalServlet - Retrieval request failed`, `NullPointerException` in `RetrievalState.updateRetrievalMetrics`) although the data is there. `RetrievalMetrics.updateMetrics` then writes the request count, the last request time and the `HashSet` of users of one PV without synchronization, and `calculateSummedMetrics` streams the map while requests update it. Observed on 2026-11-06 during the preparation of the M2 step 2 checks and on 2026-11-07 in the M2 step 5 run (`work/m34-verification/race-run50-retrieval-console-excerpt.log`: the trace from `DataRetrievalServlet.doGetSinglePV`, line 430, on the appliance built from aa-20261105-30c9aebc). The code came with upstream commit 6ca47b51 (2024-03-19) and is unchanged in archiver-appliance/epicsarchiverap, which has no issue or pull request for it (searched on 2026-11-07).

##### Scope

- Replace the map with one that is safe for concurrent use and update the entry of a PV in one atomic operation, so that a request never observes a missing entry it created.
- Synchronize the writes and the reads of one `RetrievalMetrics` (the count, the last request time, the users), so that two requests for one PV both count and the sums of the reports read consistent values.
- Keep `getRetrievalMetrics` and `getPVRetrievalMetrics` and the reports that read them unchanged in content.

Out of scope: the Analyzer's request concurrency, the retrieval memory protection and any other retrieval metric.

##### Completion Criteria

- A test in the default suite drives the real `DataRetrievalServlet` with concurrent `getData.json` requests for several archived PVs many times, and every request answers 200.
- A test updates the metrics of a few PVs from several threads at once and finds the request count of each PV equal to the number of calls; the same test fails on the code before the change.
- The retrieval metrics report still lists each PV with its request count, last retrieval time and number of users.
- The default suite passes.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- Decision Date 2026-11-06: the owner directed that this defect, found while verifying M2 step 2, is worked as its own row.
- D18 (Decision Date 2026-11-07): the row belongs to release Ahsoka; it is already worked and stands before M3.
- Decision Date 2026-11-07: the plan review confirmed the race in the map and the unsynchronized updates of one `RetrievalMetrics` (the same defect class) and the owner filed issue #78 with both in scope; the servlet test runs in the default suite without Tomcat, in the manner of `RetrievalProtectedRequestTest` (a `DataRetrievalServlet` with `ConfigServiceForTests` and proxied requests), and the deterministic regression is the count contract of the concurrent-update test, because a servlet race shows itself only by chance.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: accepted
Plan Acceptance: 2026-11-07; the owner accepted the reviewed plan (two review passes after the plan review, Open 0)
Implementation Authorization: 2026-11-07; explicit instruction to implement the accepted plan
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

1. In `src/main/org/epics/archiverappliance/retrieval/RetrievalState.java`, make `retrievalMetricsMap` a `ConcurrentHashMap` and update the entry of a PV through one atomic operation (`compute`, or `computeIfAbsent` followed by the synchronized update). In `RetrievalMetrics.java`, synchronize `updateMetrics` and the reads of an entry (its count, its last request time and its users, as one snapshot taken under the entry's lock) on that entry; `sumMetrics` and `calculateSummedMetrics` add the snapshots of the entries into the fresh accumulator of the stream, which needs no lock, and `getMetrics` and `details` read the snapshot. A count is then never lost and a report reads a consistent entry. Check: T1, T2, T3.
2. Add `RetrievalMetricsConcurrencyTest` to the default suite: several threads update the metrics of a few PVs through `updateRetrievalMetrics` many times at once, and the request count of each PV must equal its number of calls, the user set must hold every caller and the summed metrics must add up; run it once on the code before step 1 to show the failure. Check: T2.
3. Add `RetrievalConcurrentRequestTest` to the default suite, a class of its own in the manner of `RetrievalProtectedRequestTest` but without its memory protection settings, whose one percent budget would refuse concurrent requests: a `DataRetrievalServlet` with `ConfigServiceForTests`, generated samples of several PVs, and many rounds of concurrent `getData.json` requests through `doGet`; every response 200 with its samples, and after the rounds `getPVRetrievalMetrics(pv)` counts exactly the requests of each PV. Check: T1.

#### M35 - Show each selected signal in its own small canvas in a grid

Origin: bfb0dc71 / M35
Identity History: none
GitHub Issue: #79 (https://github.com/jeonghanlee/epicsarchiverap-maven/issues/79)
Status: Not started

##### Summary

The Trend of the Analyzer draws the selected signals overlaid in one plot, with one value axis for each unit; the option Split by unit stacks one panel for each unit, so signals of the same unit still overlay. A person who monitors several signals at once cannot see how each one progresses without the others in the way. This row adds an option that draws each selected signal in a small canvas of its own, in a grid (the owner's example is nine signals in three rows of three), over the same time range, beside the overlaid plot. It was asked for on 2026-11-07 (D19) and stands after the last phase of the Analyzer; the plan is a draft for the plan review.

##### Scope

- A layout option of the Trend, Overlaid (the present view and the default) or Grid, with the address parameter that carries it declared in `docs/analyzer-address.openapi.json` and written and read as the other parameters are.
- In the grid, one small canvas for each selected signal on the reusable canvas of `design-chart-canvas.md`, with its own value axis, its display name and unit, and the settings the signal already has (width, dash, gap, decimals); the time range, hover, cursors and navigation follow the common range of the page.
- The retrieval stays one request for each signal and operator as now; the grid draws from the datasets already retrieved.
- The book and the help page describe the option.

Out of scope: the analysis tools of M3 to M5; any change of retrieval; a different data source for a canvas than the selected signals; arrangements other than the grid.

##### Completion Criteria

- With the option on, each selected signal is drawn in its own canvas in a grid, and with it off the Trend is as before.
- Zoom, pan, the cursors and the pins act on the common time range of every canvas, and a signal that is hidden or removed leaves the grid.
- The option and its settings are restored from the address, and an address without them opens as before.
- A grid of nine signals is usable at the widths of 1440, 720 and 320 px without page-wide horizontal overflow.
- The book and the help page describe it, and the default suite passes.

##### Dependencies And Decisions

- M2: the reusable canvas, the toolbar, the cursors and the address of phase B (a behavioral constraint; M2 is Complete).
- M5: work ordering by the owner (D19), because M5 ends the Analyzer; nothing in M3 to M5 is a technical prerequisite.
- D19 (Decision Date 2026-11-07): the option is wanted, as a row worked after M5 and outside release Ahsoka, so that the analysis tools of that release are not held; the release that carries it is not set.
- To settle at the plan review (owner decisions): the number of canvases the grid holds and what happens beyond it; whether the canvases of one unit share a value axis range; whether a gesture on one canvas moves the time range of all; how the layout option is named in the address.

##### Implementation Plan

Plan Status: draft
Plan Acceptance: none
Implementation Authorization: none
Superseded Plan Artifacts: none

1. Review the plan and settle the owner decisions above; record the acceptance and the implementation authorization separately. Check: the decisions are in this detail.
2. Draw the grid from the retrieved datasets with one canvas for each signal on the shared canvas, and connect the common time range, the cursors and the navigation. Check: T1.
3. Add the layout option to the Options tab, the address and its declaration. Check: T2.
4. Update the book, the help page and `design-chart-canvas.md`. Check: T3.
